## Chronological
## Predecessor
 Deep Rock Galactic
Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor is a spin-off title to Deep Rock Galactic, a single player survivor-like auto-shooter developed by Funday Games and published by Ghost Ship Publishing. Wield the full arsenal of Deep Rock Galactic, take on hordes of lethal aliens, mine riches, and unlock powerful upgrades. It's one dwarf against all of Planet Hoxxes! Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor released in Steam Early Access on February 14, 2024, and released it's first 1.0 version on September 17, 2025.
The game is also available for Android devices, where players can try out a part of the game for free and unlock the rest of the game through a one-time purchase.
## Summary
DEEP ROCK GALACTIC: SURVIVOR is a single player survivor-like auto-shooter. Wield the full arsenal of Deep Rock Galactic, take on hordes of lethal aliens, mine riches, and unlock powerful upgrades. It's one dwarf against all of Planet Hoxxes!

## Key Features
- REVERSE BULLET HELL, WITH MINING
- Kill bugs, upgrade your gear, and delve ever deeper into the lethal caves of Hoxxes. Collect and assemble a devastating array of guns, unleash hell upon wave after wave of alien monsters in fast and frenetic combat, and tunnel your way to collect precious riches from deep within the cave walls. Each mission is unique, using procedural cave generation and enemy waves, just as you've come to know from Deep Rock Galactic.
- CHOOSE WHAT MISSIONS TO TAKE ON AS YOU DIG DEEP
- Delve deep, Miner! Once the Drop Pod releases you into the oppressive darkness, you are on your own. Complete the mission objectives set forth by the Company, and make it back to the Drop Pod in time to try your luck at even more deadly and lucrative encounters. Choose your route through a path of harder and harder missions while you grow ever stronger, survive all the way to the very end of your assignment, and finally get extracted alongside your hefty sack of loot.
- DEEP ROCK, FROM A WHOLE NEW PERSPECTIVE
- The universe of Deep Rock Galactic is loved by millions of players, and now you can experience it in a whole new single-player-focused way! Play each mission from a top-down perspective, navigating the caves of Hoxxes as you've never seen them before. Greybeard Deep Rock veterans will recognize much from Deep Rock Galactic, and if you are a recently joined Greenbeard: Welcome! We are thrilled to have you on board. You will like it here. Management demands it.
## Game support
Deep Rock Galactic Wiki is focused on creating the definitive guide to Deep Rock Galactic Survivor. But while we make an effort to be complete, which may include game bugs and fixes for them, we are not an official game support channel.
For official game support and bug reporting are not yet available.

# Equipment
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipment
Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor Weapons
| “ | You've proven that one dwarf can do the work of four, if we just give you enough guns.
| — Mission Control
Deep Rock Galactic provides their mining operatives with a wide variety of weapons and artifacts to help them undertake dangerous solo missions on Hoxxes IV.
Weapons are a dwarf's main line of defense and can be upgraded during a run with Upgrade Cards and Overclocks, or they can be upgraded semi-permanently between missions with Meta Upgrades. Numerous weapons and damage types become available as the player advances their classes.
Artifacts are items awarded to the dwarf throughout the run via an orbital supply drop at each stage of the dive. If activated, the supply will offer a choice of three artifact to choose from, each of those providing a chance to collect more resources, or a small conditional gameplay change (more damage while standing still, for example). Artifacts can be obtained several times during the same run, stacking their effects.
Gear are meta items kept in the player's inventory that can customize each Class Mod. Once earned, Gear will always be accessible to the player unless they're sold for Credits.
Finally, Bosco is the all-purpose drone that will accompany dwarves on all missions.
## Bosco
Main article: Survivor:Bosco
APD-B317, or Bosco, is a hovering drone that accompanies the player on all dives. He acts autonomously, targeting bugs and firing volleys of bullets on his own. His weapon can be modified by equipping a Chip.
## Weapons
The weapon the player starts with depends on the class mod they chose, and more can be chosen at levels 5, 15 and 25 during the dive. The available weapons in that choice will depend on what has been unlocked through completing milestones.
The weapons of Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor are categorized through two parameters: a default class, and tags that describe them and indicates what damage type they deal. The choice of a specific class mod will determine the starting weapon during a dive, and the other weapons will be drawn from a pool made of the class's unlocked default weapons (listed below).
Weapon tags are sorted in four categories:
- Damage type – [KINETIC] [FIRE] [ELECTRIC] [COLD] [ACID] [PLASMA]
- Family – [LIGHT] [MEDIUM] [HEAVY] [THROWABLE] [CONSTRUCT]
- Weapon type – [PROJECTILE] [EXPLOSIVE] [DRONE] [TURRET]
- Firing type – [PRECISE] [SPRAY] [AREA] [BEAM] [LASTING]
Weapons can have any number of tags in a category.

## Artifacts
Main article: Survivor:Artifacts
Artifacts are run-specific upgrades that are obtained by completing the supply drop event. During each stage of a dive, one supply drop will spawn on the map. All terrain within its radius must be dug out as indicated by a pickaxe overlay on the terrain (note: crystals and resources will not be marked with the overlay, but must still be mined).

# Gear
SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentGear
Gear menu
[Gear Thumbnail]
Gear is a new system introduced in 1.0 that adds depth and long-term progression. Occasionally, during your missions on Hoxxes, a piece of Gear will drop, and picking it up adds it to your Gear menu. There is a guaranteed Gear drop when a Dreadnought is killed in Elimination missions or after breaking the Ommoran Shell at the end of Escort Duty missions, and a smaller chance to be obtained by killing Glyphids in regular combat. Between missions, players can access the Gear menu where you can manage their loadouts for each Class Mod. The Gear Menu can hold up to 150 Gear pieces, and will remind the player to manage their Gear when there are over 86 items in the Inventory.
## Gear
Gear can be found in 4 rarities: Uncommon, Rare, Epic, and Legendary.
Depending on rarity, Gear pieces can have up to 3 levels of stats: Primary, Secondary, and Tertiary (listed in order from top down).
The rarity of the Gear determines how many of the 3 stat levels you get. Uncommon and Rare usually only have a Primary and Secondary stat, while Epic and Legendary almost always have all 3.
In addition to these stats, each Gear piece has a set of special effects they can roll called "Quirks". The game classifies these quirks as either Rare or Legendary, where Rare quirks are primarily seen on Gear that is of Rare quality or higher, and the Legendary quirks are only seen on Legendary Gear.
Quirk stat modifier values are based on rarity. Gear level does not affect the quirks in any way.
## Stat Increase Calculation
Each stat increase is calculated using the formula:
Stat Increase = StatScaling × GearScaling × (10 + Level)
where:
- StatScaling depends on the Gear type and rarity (listed in the table below).
- GearScaling is a universal multiplier applied to all Gear (the value of this multiplier varies depending if it's Additive/Flat or Multiplicative/Percentage).
- Level is the current Gear level (up to level 99).
## Types of Gear
All of the Primary, Secondary, and Tertiary stats in the following tables are represented as: Stat – (StatScaling * GearScaling).

## Gear Properties
Gears have properties, which can be divided into four categories :
- Stats, which are additive or multiplicative bonuses to your dwarf's stats.
- Quirks, which can extend the weapon's pool by letting you equip weapons of a certain tag, or give you additional stat upgrades if certain conditions are met.
- Rarity, which influences how many stat upgrades and quirks the gear has.
- Upgrades, which enhance your stat upgrades.
- Level, which influences the potency of the Stats and Quirks properties.
The Rarity of the Gear can be Uncommon, Rare, Epic, and Legendary. 
- Uncommon Gear begin with 2 Stats Upgrades and 0 Quirks.
- Rare Gear obtains an additional Quirk, for a total of 2 Stats Upgrades and 1 Quirk.
- Epic Gear obtains an additional Stats Upgrade, for a total of 3 Stats Upgrades and 1 Quirk.
- Legendary Gear obtains an additional Quirk, for a total of 3 Stats Upgrades and 2 Quirks
## Gear Level
Gear Level is a variable, from 1 to 99, which affects the base values of the Gear. The level of dropped Gear is enhanced by a variety of Milestones :
- Complete any dive on Hazard 2 • 3 • 4 • 5 (+3 Gear level for each Hazard)
- Complete a dive with 2 • 4 • 6 • 8 • 10 • 12 different Class Mods (+1 Gear level for each milestone)
- Complete 2 • 5 • 10 • 20 • 40 • 55 Masteries (+1 Gear level for each milestone)
- Fully complete 2 • 4 • 6 • 8 • 10 • 15 •20 • 25 • 30 • 35 • 40 • 50 mission nodes (+1 Gear level for each milestone)
- Get 2 • 4 • 6 • 8 • 10 • 15 • 20 • 25 • 30 • 40 weapons to level 18 (+1 Gear level for each milestone)
- Deal 2,000,000 • 10,000,000 • 100,000,000 • 1,000,000,000 [ACID]/[COLD]/[ELECTRICAL]/[FIRE]/[KINETIC]/[PLASMA] damage (+1 Gear level for each milestone, for every damage type)
- Reach player rank 4 • 8 • 12 • 16 • 20 • 24 • 28 • 32 • 36 • 40 (+1 Gear level for each milestone)
The Gear level bonus is displayed at all times in the Gear Menu.
Gear level is something that cannot be upgraded on a piece of Gear in the Inventory per se; however, Upgrades can be applied to any Gear, for a total of 3 times per piece, via the Gear Menu, and enhances even further the Stats Upgrades.
Each upgrade given to a piece of Gear in the Upgrade tab gives it 4 levels under the hood.
## Trivia
- Smart Equip doesn't take into account the values of the stats, or the relevance of a gear piece to the class mod used. Instead, it converts the Gear Level, Upgrade Level, and Rarity into a numeric value, and just pick the higher value for each slot.
- The numeric value is calculated as follows:
Smart Equip Value = Gear Level + (Upgrade Level * 5) + (Rarity (as integer) * 15)
- The first completion of a specific Vanguard Contract and Lethal Operation will grant you a guaranteed Epic / Legendary piece of Gear, respectively. Since the Gear is dropped on boss death, it's possible to collect the piece of Gear, end the run by taking too much damage, and restart the dive for another piece of Gear of guaranteed quality.
- Although the developers stated that upgrading a gear piece internally grants 5 levels, each upgrade provides only 4 levels.
## References
- ↑ John | Funday: Smart Equip formula. #ask-the-devs
- ↑ Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor. Patch 3: Mid-game saves and more!
- ↑ John | Funday: "...each upgrade adds 5 levels to the piece you are upgrading". #ask-the-devs
## Nav

=====
# Weapons
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeapons
For a page with similar information, see Survivor:Equipment
## Weapon Tags
Every weapon has a number of descriptive tags associated with it. Here these tags are categorized into damage type, weapon family, weapon type and firing type. Weapons can have any number of tags in a category. Some weapons can have their tags changed mid-dive by applying special Overclocks to them.
Damage type
- [KINETIC] – basic non-elemental damage.
- [FIRE] – inflicts Burn.
- [ELECTRICAL] – inflicts Shock.
- [COLD] – inflicts Slow.
- [ACID] – inflicts Corrode.
- [PLASMA] – projectiles bounce off walls and bugs.
Weapon family
- [LIGHT]
- [MEDIUM]
- [HEAVY]
- [THROWABLE]
- [CONSTRUCT]
Weapon type
- [PROJECTILE] – typically a weapon that fires bullets.
- [EXPLOSIVE] – weapons that shoot explosives, and grenades.
- [DRONE] – constructs that fly around and attack hostiles.
- [TURRET] – constructs that are (typically) immobile.
- [GROUNDZONE] - weapons create a zone on the ground that damages enemies and applies elemental stacks.
Firing type
- [PRECISE] – damage is cast along a predictable line.
- [SPRAY] – damage is cast into a cone.
- [AREA] – damage is dealt in an area.
- [BEAM] – damage is cast along a line or cone.
- [LASTING] – weapon has some effect that remains in the world for a duration, like a construct or a damage puddle.
Special tags
These tags are obtained by upgrading weapons with certain overclocks, and enable Tag Mastery Upgrades when at least two weapons share the same tag:
- [AKIMBO] - Gained by the Akimbo overclocks.
- [SIDEARM] - Gained by the Sidearm overclocks.
- [THE FAVOURITE] - Gained by The Favourite overclocks.
- [THICK BOY] - Gained by the Thick Boy overclocks.
## Explanation of Stats
- Clip Size: How many projectiles a weapon can fire before reloading.
- Damage: Damage inflicted per hit from a weapon.
- Fire Rate: Projectiles fired per second, and how fast a clip is emptied.
- Piercing: How many enemies a projectile can pierce. Larger enemies, Elites, and Bosses require higher piercing values.
- Potency: Amount of status effect stacks applied by [FIRE] [ELECTRICAL] [COLD] and [ACID] weapons.
- Reload Time: Downtime after a clip is emptied before it can be fired again; affects speed of deploying [CONSTRUCT] weapons.
- Drones and Charges: The amount of drones/turrets/etc. the player can keep in their pocket at a time. When the player stands still, charges are deployed one after another; The last charge is auto-deployed if charges would over-fill.
- Lifetime: How long [LASTING] weapons remain active. For [BEAM] weapons, this stat determines how long it takes to empty the clip - replacing the 'Fire Rate' stat.
- Beams: Number of beams for [BEAM] weapons.
- Explosion Radius: Maximum radius covered by [EXPLOSIVE] weapons.
- Weapon Range: Distance covered by [BEAM] weapons, and targeting range for [PROJECTILE] weapons.
(Note that projectile range is determined by hidden stats for projectile lifetime and speed, which regular Weapon Range upgrades do not affect. However, certain overclocks that affect Weapon Range such as High Velocity Bullets do modify these hidden stats, including the targeting range.)
The player is given a choice of upgrades to their stats every time they level up during a dive, as well as from the store where nitra can be spent on gun-specific improvements. When a stat is modified from its base value, it follows the formula
Stat = ((Base * Skill [icon Modifier Weapons]) + Flat [icon Modifier Flat]) * Meta [icon Modifier Meta] * Overclock [icon Modifier Overclocks] * Artifact [icon Modifier Artifacts]
...where Base is a weapon's base stat, Skill [icon Modifier Weapons] is the sum of all modifiers from Mid-dive Upgrades, Flat [icon Modifier Flat] is the sum of all flat (non-percentile) modifiers (like charge or beam count upgrades), Meta [icon Modifier Meta] is the product of Meta Upgrades * Class modifiers * Weapon Mastery modifiers, and Overclock [icon Modifier Overclocks] and Artifact [icon Modifier Artifacts] are the products of all Overclock and Artifacts modifiers. These calculations can be viewed for equipped weapons from the pause screen during a dive.
## Class Defaults
Each class has default access to a set of 10 or 11 total weapons, unlocking them as the player levels up in that class. Once unlocked they can always be found during dives while playing that class.
| Class | Starting defaults | Unlockable defaults

In addition to their defaults, certain sub-classes have access to all weapons of a given tag:
- Recon (Scout) has access to his default set, plus all [LIGHT] weapons.
- Weapons Specialist (Gunner) has access to his default set, plus all [PROJECTILE] weapons.
- Heavy Gunner (Gunner) has access to his default set, plus all [HEAVY] weapons.
- Interrogator (Driller) has access to his default set, plus all [FIRE] and [ACID] weapons.
- Strong Armed (Driller) has access to his default set, plus all [THROWABLE] weapons.
- Maintenance Worker (Engineer) has access to his default set, plus all [CONSTRUCT] weapons.
- Demolitionist (Engineer) has access to his default set, plus all [EXPLOSIVE] weapons.
- Gridrunner (Demolisher) has access to his default set, plus all [ELECTRICAL] weapons.
- Operator (Demolisher) has access to his default set, plus all [DRONE] weapons.
Some Gear allow additional access to weapons given their tags:
- DW40K adds all [BEAM] weapons to the drop pool.
- Strange Mixture adds all [DRONE] weapons to the drop pool.
- Support Chip adds two random damage types to the drop pool.
- Sniper Scope adds all [PROJECTILE] weapons to the drop pool.

# Class Mods
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesClass Mods
Class Mods are sub-classes that give the dwarves unique abilities and stat boosts which further tailor their playstyle.
## Scout Class Mods
Main class: Survivor:Scout
The Classic subclass for Scout is the first subclass in the game, being the only one available to first-time players. This class mod gets a bonus to max HP and Move Speed, both practical and useful stats. His starting weapon is the Deepcore GK2, a balanced, straightforward projectile weapon.
The Recon has the stats to pull off narrow escapes and survive bugs in close proximity. He starts off with the Zhukov NUK17s, a gun that fires in four directions at once, which naturally fills its potential when the dwarf is surrounded on all sides. The subclass is unlocked at Scout Rank 09.
The Sharp Shooter specializes in dealing critical hits, and combined with his passive effect of releasing a shrapnel explosion on overkill damage, this class mod encourages the use of precise slow-firing, high-damage weapons. He starts with the M1000 Classic, an incredibly powerful rifle whose high-caliber rounds can pierce 30 grunts. The subclass is unlocked at Scout Rank 18.
| Class Mod | Description | Starting weapon
| [Class Mod Classic]Classic | +10% Move Speed+15% HP | [GK2] Deepcore GK2
| [Class Mod Recon]Recon | Gives access to [LIGHT] weapons+25% DodgeWhen dodging enemy damage: +35% Move Speed, +35% Reload Speed for 5 seconds. | [Zhukovs] Zhukov NUK17
| [Class Mod Sharpshooter] Sharp Shooter | Releases a shrapnel explosion when doing overkill damage+15% Critical Chance50% Critical Damage | [M1000] M1000 Classic
## Gunner Class Mods
Main class: Survivor:Gunner
The Weapons Specialist subclass for Gunner starts with the "Lead Storm" Powered Minigun, a weapon that can only fire forwards. This class mod is able to equip all [PROJECTILE] weapons, giving him a wide but diluted weapon pool, and favors rapid-fire projectile weapons due to his passive of firing a burst of projectiles for every 100 projectiles fired. The Weapons Specialist is the only Gunner subclass available when the player first unlocks Gunner at Player Rank 03.
The Juggernaut is a high-resistance class mod which thrives in the thick of it, as this class mod starts with extra Armor and gains a stacking damage boost every time damage is taken. His starting weapon is the "Bulldog" Heavy Revolver, a no-nonsense, heavy-hitting gun with moderate piercing. Juggernaut has a tighter pool of available weapons, so there's a greater likelihood the player finds a particular set of Gunner weapons. The subclass is unlocked at Gunner Rank 09.
The Heavy Gunner starts with the "Thunderhead" Heavy Autocannon, a weapon whose firepower entirely depends on being pointed in the right direction the moment it's ready. Unlike the Powered Minigun, the Heavy Autocannon can aim any direction, but once it starts firing it can't be turned. The subclass also offers improvements to all [HEAVY] weapons. The subclass is unlocked at Gunner Rank 18.
| Class Mod | Description | Starting weapon
| [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist | For every 100 projectiles fired, you shoot out 8 high powered projectiles in all directionsGives access to [PROJECTILE] weapons | [Leadstorm] "Lead Storm" Powered Minigun
| [Class Mod Juggernaut]Juggernaut | +10 Armor+50 Max HP-50% Weapon RangeWhen you take damage you gain +10% Damage for 10 seconds. Stacks up to 5 times. | [Bulldog] "Bulldog" Heavy Revolver
| [Class Mod Heavy Gunner]Heavy Gunner | Gives access to [HEAVY] weapons-10% Move Speed+ [HEAVY] 25% Weapon Range+ [HEAVY] 25% Reload Speed | [AutoCannon] "Thunderhead" Heavy Autocannon
## Engineer Class Mods
Main class: Survivor:Engineer
The Maintenance Worker is the default unlocked class mod for the Engineer, unlocked at Rank 5. He specializes in [CONSTRUCT] weapons such as turrets and drones. His starting weapon is the LMG Gun Platform, and autonomous turret gun which is deployed by standing still.
The Tinkerer is a subclass proficient with all weapons. He starts with increased XP Gain and extra weapon levels, allowing him to unlock weapon Overclocks faster. His starting weapon is the "Warthog" Auto 210, a well-rounded [KINETIC] shotgun. The subclass is unlocked at Engineer Rank 09.
The Demolitionist specializes in explosive weapons, blowing away packs of glyphids and even rocks and stones with area damage. Additionally, he is able to equip all [EXPLOSIVE] weapons. He starts with a Deepcore PGL, which are loaded with grenades that pack a punch. The subclass is unlocked at Engineer Rank 18.
| Class Mod | Description | Starting weapon
| [Class Mod Maintenance Worker]Maintenance Worker | Gives access to [CONSTRUCT] weapons+ [CONSTRUCT] 10% Damage+ [CONSTRUCT] 10% Reload Speed | [LMG Turret] LMG Gun Platform
| [Class Mod Tinkerer]Tinkerer | Weapons start at weapon level 3+10% XP Gain | [Warthog] "Warthog" Auto 210
| [Class Mod Demolitionist]Demolitionist | Gives access to [EXPLOSIVE] weapons+ [EXPLOSIVE] 20% Explosion Radius+ [EXPLOSIVE] 20% Reload Speed | [Deepcore PGL] Deepcore PGL
## Driller Class Mods
Main class: Survivor:Driller
The Foreman is the Driller's default starting class, unlocked at Rank 7. He gains a bonus to mining speed that ramps up as you dig through more rocks and stones, making busting through walls a breeze. His starting weapon is the Subata 120, which fires behind the Driller, covering his behind as he digs through walls.
The Interrogator is a damage over time specialist. He has a significant penalty to damage, but has greatly increased status effect damage, making damage types like Fire and Acid more lethal. Additionally, he is able to equip all [FIRE] and [ACID] weapons. His starting weapon is the CRSPR Flamethrower, melting away glyphids that get too close. The subclass is unlocked at Driller Rank 9.
The Strong Armed can equip all [THROWABLE] weapons, and his strong arms lets him target enemies from further away due to his increased Weapon Range. He starts with the Impact Axe, which creates a kinetic shockwave on impact and are called back to the wielder after throwing them all. The subclass is unlocked at Driller Rank 18.
| Class Mod | Description | Starting weapon
| [Class Mod Foreman]Foreman | When mining gain +2% Mining Speed for 2 seconds. Stacks up to 25 times. | [Subata] Subata 120
| [Class Mod Interrogator]Interrogator | Gives access to [FIRE] [ACID] weapons.+100% Status Effect Damage-30% Damage | [CRSPR] CRSPR Flamethrower
| [Class Mod Strong Armed]Strong Armed | Gives access to [THROWABLE] weapons.+20% Weapon Range | [Impact Axe] Impact Axe
## Demolisher Class Mods
Main class: Survivor:Demolisher
A relatively new addition to the workforce, the Demolisher drives a Rockdozer, giving him an unconventional method of movement compared to walking on two legs.
The Contractor is an all-rounder class mod when it comes to leaving a trail of devastation in the caves of Hoxxes. He stats with the Dragonstorm Incinerator, a flamethrower that shoots fiery beams from behind. The Contractor is unlocked at Player Rank 9
The Gridrunner is a class mod specialized in handling [ELECTRICAL] weaponry. With enhanced Potency, he maximizes the efficiency of status effect weapons to dominate the battlefield. The Gridrunner is unlocked at Class Rank 9.
The Operator is a specialized class mod designed for [DRONE] warfare. With an increased Lifetime stat, he ensures sustained battlefield presence for all deployable drones. The Operator is unlocked at Class Rank 18.

# Driller  (alias/duplicados: Foreman, Interrogator, Strong Armed)
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesDriller
| “ | The Driller likes to get up close and personal, burning his way through hordes with chemical weapons and mining his way through walls with his trusty powerdrills!
| — Class description
The Driller is one of five playable characters in DRG Survivor, unlocking at Player Rank 7.
The Driller Class
Driller boasts the fastest mining speed in the game, and the most elemental weapons of any class. His starting weapons are the CRSPR Flamethrower, Subata 120, High Explosive Grenade, and Krakatoa Sentinel. His class mods are Foreman, Interrogator, and Strong Armed, with Foreman being unlocked by default.
## Stats
Baseline stats for the Driller Class are below. Stats may be altered by upgrades or by choosing a Class Mod.
Starting Stats
| Health | 145
| Critical Chance | 5%
| Critical Damage | 150%
| Mining Speed | +20%
## Available Weapons
Driller has a set of eleven weapons that, once unlocked by ranking up in the class, can always be found by him during regular dives.
The Interrogator Driller also has access to all unlocked [FIRE] and [ACID] weapons, and the Strong Armed Driller has access to all [THROWABLE] weapons.

# Gunner  (alias/duplicados: Heavy Gunner, Juggernaut, Weapons Specialist)
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesGunner
| “ | Who needs fancy tricks like speed or gadgetry when you can just apply a stupendous amount of heavy firepower to any problem? That's what the Gunner is all about
| — Class description
The Gunner is one of five playable classes in DRG Survivor, unlocked by reaching Player Rank 3.
The Gunner Class
Gunner's kit consists almost entirely of projectile weapons, with a few fire weapons alongside for more AOE damage. His starting weapons consist of the "Lead Storm" Powered Minigun, "Bulldog" Heavy Revolver, and Incendiary Grenade, making them the only class to not begin with four weapons. His class mods are Weapons Specialist, Juggernaut, and Heavy Gunner, with Weapons Specialist being available to the player upon unlocking the class for the first time. 
## Stats
Baseline stats for the Gunner Class are below. Stats may be altered by upgrades or by choosing a Class Mod.
Starting Stats
| Health | 160
| Armor | 10
| Critical Chance | 5%
| Critical Damage | 150%
## Available Weapons
Gunner has a set of ten weapons that, once unlocked by ranking up in the class, can always be found by him during regular dives.
The Weapons Specialist Gunner has an enormous list of available weapons, having access to all unlocked [PROJECTILE] weapons. The Heavy Gunner has access to all unlocked [HEAVY] weapons.

## Weapons Specialist passive
The damage dealt by the passive is calculated the same way as shown in the weapon details screen, which is:
Stat = ((Base * Skill [icon Modifier Weapons]) + Flat [icon Modifier Flat]) * Meta [icon Modifier Meta] * Overclock [icon Modifier Overclocks] * Artifact [icon Modifier Artifacts] * Mutator [icon Modifier Mutators]
The base damage of each projectile is 100 damage. The upgrades that affect the projectiles are:
- [Damage] Body Shots upgrades, [PROJECTILE] damage upgrades, and [KINETIC] damage upgrades, which go into the Skill bucket.
- The Damage upgrades (not Crit Damage or Status Effect Damage) on Gear, Meta Upgrades, and Masteries, which go into the Meta bucket.
- Artifacts that grant Damage, which go into the Artifact bucket.
- Mutators that may reduce damage, which go into the Mutator bucket.
Notice that there is nothing for the Overclock bucket, as the passive cannot gain an Overclock.
After the calculation is done, the game then adds an additional 100 damage to the final calculated damage.
These projectiles are able to crit, and their piercing value is 100. Their piercing is not affected by Gear or Artifacts.

# Meta Upgrades  (alias/duplicados: Upgrades)
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsMeta Upgr.
[Meta Upgrades Thumbnail]
All Meta Upgrades available
Meta Upgrades are semi-permanent stat buffs that apply to all classes. They can be purchased from the main menu using Credits and Resources.
Non-permanent upgrades that improve many of the same stats can be selected from random cards in the middle of a dive.
As part of the 1.0 Update, Morkite Reactor Cores have been deleted from the game and are no longer needed to enhance meta-upgrades.
Meta upgrades
| Name | Stat upgraded | Description | Mineral cost
| [Health] Getting Fit | Max HP | Increases your Max HP | [Ressource Bismor] Bismor
| [Damage] Heavy Bullets | Damage | Increases your damage | [Ressource Bismor] Bismor
| [Reload Speed] Reload Speed | Reload Speed | Increases your weapons' reload speed | [Ressource Bismor] Bismor
| [Armor] Upgraded Armor | Armor | Decreases the amount of damage you take from all sources | [Ressource Croppa] Croppa
| [Mining Speed] Mining 101 | Mining Speed | Increases your mining speed | [Ressource Croppa] Croppa

## Nav

=====
# Level-up improvements  (alias/duplicados: Mid-dive Upgrades)
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsUpgr. Cards
[Upgrades]
This page documents the mid-dive stat upgrade cards that are offered when the player levels up. These only last until the end of the dive.
Stats can also be improved on the main menu with semi-permanent meta upgrades.
## Upgrade Cards
The player is given a choice of these every time they level up during a dive. They only last as long as the mission does.
Stacking multiple bonuses from level-up improvements is always additive; for example, two 50% bonuses are effectively a 100% increase from the base value, not 225% (1.5 × 1.5). Overclocks on the other hand do mix multipliers with additive values.
## Weapon Upgrades
These are only applied to one weapon at a time, depicted on the card. Nitra can be spent at the store to purchase these.
Taking these increases a weapon's level, usually by one. At weapon levels 6, 12 and 18 the player is given a choice of Overclocks for that weapon. The Paint Job upgrade's only purpose is for Overclocking a weapon sooner.
| Name | Effect | Weapon level increase | Available to
| [Subata] [Punch] Add Punch | + -/-/25%/50%/- piercing | Increase weapon level by 1 | All [PROJECTILE] weapons (except Experimental Plasma Charger)
| [Subata] [Damage] Bigger Cogs | + 10%/15%/25%/35%/50% damage | Increase weapon level by 1 | All weapons
| [Subata] [Reload Speed] Loosen Bolts | + 10%/15%/25%/35%/50% reload speed | Increase weapon level by 1 | All weapons
| [Fire Drone] [Drone Count] More Drones! | +1 extra drones (always legendary) | Increase weapon level by 1 | All [DRONE] weapons
| [CRSPR] [Beam damage] Open Valves | + –/–/10%/15%/25% weapon range | Increase weapon level by 1 | All [BEAM] weapons
| [Subata] [Paintjob] Paint Job | Lets you imagine your weapon is now a lot prettier | Increase weapon level by 2 (rare) / 3 (epic) | All weapons
| [CRSPR] [Beam Count] Split Nozzles | +1 beams (always legendary) | Increase weapon level by 1 | All [BEAM] weapons (except Colette Wave Cooker)
| [Subata] [Fire Rate] Tighten Springs | + 10%/15%/25%/35%/50% fire rate | Increase weapon level by 1 | All [PROJECTILE] weapons
| [Subata] [Potency] Tweak Potency | + -/15%/25%/35%/- potencyIncrease the amount of status effect stacks applied | Increase weapon level by 1 | All [ACID], [ELECTRICAL] and [FIRE] weapons, and any weapon granted a status effect via an Overclock
Percentages are in order of common/uncommon/rare/epic/legendary
## Tag Mastery Upgrades
These are applied to all weapons that fit a given tag. Additionally, these upgrades are only offered to players who carry at least two weapons with matching tags.
For example, if you have at least two [PROJECTILE] weapons, the game will start to offer its corresponding Tag Mastery upgrades listed below. Conversely, if a player has only one [BEAM] weapon, the game will not offer its corresponding Tag Mastery upgrades until they obtain another [BEAM] weapon. 
These upgrades can be obtained during level-ups, or bought at the shop using Gold.
| Name | Effect | Game description
| [Beam damage] Apply Dwarf Tape | [BEAM]:+ –/10%/20%/30%/40% lifetime | Increases the expected lifetime of all [BEAM] weapons
| [Duration] Apply Dwarf Tape | [LASTING]:+ –/–/10%/15%/20% lifetime | Increases the expected lifetime of all [LASTING] weapons
| [Duration] Apply Dwarf Tape | [DRONE]:+ –/10%/20%/30%/40% lifetime | Increases the expected lifetime of all [DRONE] weapons

## Player Stat Upgrades
These are generally applied to the dwarf and all his weapons. Gold can be spent at the store to purchase these.
| Name | Effect | Game description
| [Damage] Body Shots | + –/5%/10%/15%/20% damage | Increases the damage of all your weapons
| [Crit Chance] Careful Aim | + 3%/6%/9%/12%/15% crit chance | Increases the chance to get a Critical Hit, dealing bonus damage
| [Mining Speed] Dwarven Heritage | + –/5%/10%/15%/25% mining speed | Increases how fast you can mine rock and stone
| [Armor] Extra Padding | + –/3/6/9/12 armor | Increases resistance to all sources of damage
| [Health] Extra Rations | + 15/30/45/60/80 max HP | Increases how much damage you can take before dying
| [Reload Speed] Fast Hands | + 3%/6%/9%/12%/18% reload speed | Decreases the time to reload all your weapons
| [Move Speed] Fast Little Legs | +2%/4%/6%/9%/12% move speed | Increases how fast you can move
| [Luck] Getting Lucky | +5 (rare) / +10 (epic) luck | Increases the chance to roll higher rarities
| [XP Gain] Learn on the Job | + -/-/10%/15%/20% XP gain | Increases the XP gained during a dive
| [Crit Damage] Mind Blowing | + 5%/10%/15%/25%/50% critical damage | Increases the damage bonus of a Critical Hit
| [Pickup Radius] Pocket Magnets | + -/15%/30%/45%/60% pickup radius | —
| [Damage Status Effect] Offensive Chemistry | + -/10%/20%/30%/40% status effect damage | Increases all damage over time from [ACID], [ELECTRICAL] and [FIRE]
Percentages are in order of common/uncommon/rare/epic/legendary
## Using Gold and Nitra
The shop between dive stages (Prices modified by DRG Coupon)
In between dive stages, a selection of the above improvement cards becomes available for purchase at the drop pod at the cost of Gold and Nitra. Nitra can be used to purchase weapon-specific improvements and to reroll Artifact offerings at the Supply Pod (once the Artifact Rerolls Meta Upgrades has been purchased, while Gold can be used to purchase general improvements and heal 50% at the drop pod, to re-roll the card selection at the shop, and to re-roll the card selection on leveling up.
Upgrade costs between dive stages
| Card rarity | Weapon | Tag mastery | Player stat
| Uncommon | 14 [Ressource Nitra] | 29 [Ressource Gold] | 23 [Ressource Gold]
| Rare | 18 [Ressource Nitra] | 35 [Ressource Gold] | 29 [Ressource Gold]
| Epic | 24 [Ressource Nitra] | 48 [Ressource Gold] | 40 [Ressource Gold]
| Legendary | 38 [Ressource Nitra] | 77 [Ressource Gold] | 63 [Ressource Gold]
50% heal ([Ressource Gold]): 30
Reroll ([Ressource Gold]): 5, 7, 10, 14, 20, 28, 39, 55, 77, 108 ...
## Notable Quirks
During Weapon Mastery missions, some Player Stat Upgrades become over-valued in terms of card rarity, due to the fact that the player has only one weapon. The stats that are over-valued are those that affect "all" weapons: Critical Chance, Critical Damage, Damage, Reload Speed and Status Effect Damage. e.g., Bigger Cogs for the Subata 120, which gives +25% Damage (Rare), ranks lower than the Body Shots that gives +20% Damage (Legendary), even though the Subata-specific upgrade effectively delivers a higher percentage.
However, it is worth nothing that Critical Chance and Critical Damage retain their value as separate multipliers to your damage, and Status Effect Damage and Body Shots retain their value since they increase Status Effect Damage whereas Weapon Upgrades do not.
It's also worth noting that not all upgrades will increase all damage types:
- Bigger Cogs upgrades do not increase status effect damage, except for the Bigger Cogs upgrade for the matching element ([FIRE], [ACID], or [ELECTRICAL])
- Status effect damage cannot crit, so it is unaffected by Critical Chance and Critical Damage upgrades
- Status effect damage upgrades do not increase damage dealt by direct hits from a weapon - they only increase the damage dealt by the status effects applied by those direct hits

# Overclock  (alias/duplicados: Overclocks)
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsOverclocks
[Overclocks Thumbnail]
| “ | Overclocks – My favourite type of clock!
| — Dwarf
Overclocks are specialized weapon upgrades that can be activated by leveling weapons up during a dive. Balanced overclocks can be selected for a weapon at Weapon Levels 6 and 12, and a powerful "Unstable" overclock can be selected at Weapon Level 18. Weapons can be leveled up by focusing on weapon-specific upgrades during a dive.

# Masteries  (alias/duplicados: Mastery)
Masteries are special dives that grant permanent stat upgrades upon completion. Completing Mastery Missions also awards Mastery Points, which give way to unlocking Anomaly Dives. There are three kinds of Masteries: Weapon Masteries, Class Mod Masteries, and Biome Masteries.
Mastery Points ([Mastery Point]) are rewarded based on the highest Hazard Level the player has completed a mastery mission on.
## Weapon Masteries
| “ | One weapon only, just like in the good old days. Let's see if you can keep up.
| — Mission Control
Weapon Masteries are short, three-stage dives where players make use of only a single weapon. They are unlocked one weapon at a time by upgrading that weapon to level 18 in any dive, and having unlocked its corresponding Hazard Level requirement, which is listed in the Weapon Mastery menu. Completing a Weapon Mastery will permanently upgrade that weapon's stats.
Rules for Weapon Masteries
- 1 weapon only
- 3 stages
- Class and biome restricted by weapon selection
- Minimum Hazard requirement (2 to 4) imposed by weapon selection
Bonus granted for weapons with one mastery stat
+12% to one stat
Bonus granted for weapons with two mastery stats
+7% to one stat, +7% to the other

## Class Mod Masteries
| “ | I'm the masterclass class master!
| — Dwarf
Class Mod Masteries are challenges that upon completion award the player with permanent boosts to their stats for that class mod. The dives have five stages, and featuring smaller maps with no secondary objectives. Additionally, there is no Red Sugar, no Life Regen, and no healing on level up. The only way to restore health is to purchase it in the shop between stages, and by obtaining the Tactical Cookie artifact.
These Mastery missions become unlocked once the player completes any dive on Hazard 2 or higher using a given Class Mod. Completing these Masteries permanently enhances that Class Mod's stats.
Rules for Class Mod Masteries
- Smaller map
- No secondary objectives
- No Red Sugar, no Life Regen, no heal on level up
- Starts with two DRG Coupons (Combined, shop prices have a 40% discount)
- Unavailable artifacts: Red Sugar Cube, Vita-Miner Pills
- Biome restricted by Class Mod selection
- Minimum Hazard requirement (2 to 4) imposed by Class Mod selection

## Biome Masteries
| “ | The dive is so nice, you do it twice!
| — Dwarf
Biome Masteries are extra long (ten-stage) dives with otherwise normal rules. To unlock a biome's Mastery Mission, any dive must be completed in that Biome on Hazard 3 or higher.
Rules for Biome Masteries
- 10 stages instead of 5
- Dreadnoughts on stages 5 and 10
- Stages 6–9 have 2 elites
- Hazard 3+
Stats improved for Crystalline Caverns
+15% XP Gain
Stats improved for Magma Core
+8% XP Gain, +8 Armor
Stats improved for Hollow Bough
+8% XP Gain, +20% Pickup Radius
Stats improved for Salt Pits
+8% XP Gain, +8% Mining Speed
Stats improved for Azure Weald
+8% XP Gain, +8 Luck
Stats improved for Glacial Strata
+8% XP Gain, +8% Reload Speed
## History
Masteries used to be the only way to obtain Morkite Reactor Cores, which were resources that were used for Meta Upgrades. As per 1.0, the requirement for Morkite Cores has been removed from the game, as well as the resource itself.
Masteries used to give a bonus that was incremental with the highest Hazard completed before 1.0. This progression path was deemed too tedious by the developers, and as per 1.0, Masteries offer no direct benefit from being completed in a higher Hazard, but the points awarded can still be used to unlock Anomaly Dives, as well as Steam Achievements for completing Biome & Weapon Masteries in Hazard 5.

# Milestones
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesMilestones
[Milestone Thumbnail]
Milestones are the primary method to unlock new gameplay in Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor, be it new classes or subclasses, weapons and overclocks for them, or game changing artifacts, completing them is the way to unlock more options to explore Hoxxes (and rain down fire and hell on its denizens).
You can consult the milestones, their requirements, progress and rewards from the milestone menu (available from the main screen and the pause menu).

## Biome Goals
Any Biome Goals that were completed by the end of a dive are listed under "Milestones" for the completion report. Finishing Biome Goals unlocks more hazardous biomes by awarding the player Biome Points.
## History
All the weapons in the game used to be provided without overclocks available, and would need to be brought up to weapon level 12 during a run to unlock overclocks for subsequent runs. A seperate milestone for each weapon would be completed when it first reached the level 12.

Squish the bugs - for Karl!
## Basic Units
Mobile enemies:
- Tier 1 Grunts (brown), Tier 2 Grunts (blue) and Tier 3 Grunts (reddish) are basic melee enemies who will constantly walk towards the player, dealing damage when within biting range. They come in three colors: brown, blue and reddish brown. The sole difference is that blue and reddish brown grunts have more health than brown grunts.
- Fast Grunts are very similar to regular grunts, except they are considerably faster and have less health than their cousins.
- Slashers are upgraded melee enemies, featuring more health and higher damage than Grunts.
- Acid Spitters keep a medium distance from the player and fire purple acid shots. Spitters will not approach to melee, and will retreat when the player gets too near.
- Exploders are larger, orange Glyphids who will approach the player and then explode in a large AOE. They can be killed from a distance to trigger their explosion, or a careful player can dodge in and out of their trigger range to cause them to explode instantly. Exploders are very valuable for clearing swarms of enemies, as their explosion will damage other bugs. Most swarm events will spawn medium to large waves of Exploders that can herded into groups to trigger chain reactions.
- Mini Exploders are smaller, purple cousins of the Exploders that exclusively spawn in the Azure Weald. They are much faster and more numerous than regular Exploders, but are much weaker in terms of both health and damage, making them a much less reliable way to clear out swarms.
- Praetorians are large melee glyphids that are much more durable than the other bugs, and can slowly dig out terrain in their pursuit of the player.
- Big Praetorians are an even larger, blue variant of the Praetorian that spawn exclusively in the Azure Weald. Much like Blue Grunts, the only notable difference compared to regular Praetorians is in their health.
- Mactera are flying melee enemies who can passively fly over terrain. Walls, vines, and voids mean nothing to Mactera, who fly together in small clusters in the Hollow Bough, only hurting the player should you run directly into them.
- Q'ronar Younglings are melee enemies that are exclusive to the Salt Pits. They travel aimlessly, bouncing off of the walls and hurting the player should they run into you.
Stationary enemies:
- Stabber Vines are stationary melee enemies, attacking the player should you get close. The attacks aren't predictive, so the greatest danger lies in being too slow or standing still.
- Spitball Infectors are stationary ranged enemies that fire large green shots at the player.
## Mini Elites
Mini Elite are stronger purple varieties who spawn in swarms or sometimes in the background trickle. They stand out as much stronger opponents compared to the dozens of weaker bugs surrounding them. They are purple in color and have a small health bar.
- Grunt Mini Elites deal and take more damage than their fodder counterparts, and they are much faster.
- Fast Grunt Mini Elites deal and take more damage than their fodder counterparts, and they are much faster.
- Slasher Mini Elites deal and take more damage than their fodder counterparts, and they are much faster.
- Spitter Mini Elites shoot a spread of three shots rather than just one. As with all other spitters, they retreat when the player approaches them.
- Mactera Mini Elites will actively pursue the player like the Elite Mactera, but they have less health.
- Q'ronar Youngling Mini Elites can break the soft terrain of the Salt Pits while rolling, only bouncing off of minerals and hard stone. They also have a slight ability to steer themselves towards the player.
- Wardens are a unique champion exclusive to the Azure Weald. They keep their distance from the player and will occasionally stop moving to create a mind link, giving all enemies in the radius considerable damage reduction.
## Bosses
Elite Enemies serve as the boss encounters for every stage but the last, sporting increased health, speed, and damage compared to the other bugs on the field. Four elites also spawn inside eggs on the final stage of a dive, needing to be hatched before the Dreadnought can be summoned. All but one of the elite enemies are variations of pre-existing enemies.
- Elite Slashers and Elite Praetorians can break terrain much more effectively than regular Praetorians.
- Elite Acid Spitters shoot five shots in a spread pattern. They are just as passive as their non-elite counterparts, repositioning themselves when the player gets too close.
- Elite Mactera will actively pursue the player, unlike their fodder brethren who idly fly about. This can make them dangerous while the player is digging through terrain, as most of the attacks will be blocked by the rocks and stones before they can hit the mactera.
- Elite Q'ronar Shellbacks are unique in several different ways. They function similarly to the Q'ronar Younglings, but when near the player will stop to fire a beam of acid at you, dealing considerable damage. They can only spawn on Hazard 2 and above, and only in the Crystalline Caverns, Magma Core, and Salt Pits.
The Dreadnought spawns at the last stage of the dive inside a cocoon, and acts as the final boss for that dive. There are two different Dreadnought enemies that can be encountered, both of which are keen on dismembering whoever had the gall to disturb their slumber.
- The Regular Dreadnought towers over all the other enemies in the game, and is the single most dangerous of them all. Its main attack is a long distance leap which deals massive damage. While it is telegraphed, making sure you have some amount of movement speed is ideal to make sure you don't die to it. The two other attacks it has is where it smashes the ground, creating a funnel of rock pillars which will confine you to a small area until you dig out, as well as an attack where the Dreadnought fires swarm eggs at the player, which can hatch into grunts, slashers, and praetorians.
- The Dreadnought Twins are a dual boss encounter, featuring the purple, melee-focused Lacerator; and the orange, range-focused Arbalest. The Lacerator deals melee damage upon running into the player, but also shares the leaping attack that the Regular Dreadnought has. The Arbalest keeps its distance from the player, shooting a spread of fireballs in your direction. Each twin has its own health bar, and when the difference in health becomes too large, they will heal each other so that their health bars are equal. Thus, the main challenge in the Twins fight is making sure you damage each of them to a similar extent to minimize the amount of times they heal.
## Passive Creatures
- Lootbugs are passive creatures that mind their own business, not bothering to pursue the player. Getting within mining range allows you to attack the Lootbug, and killing it gifts you with some gold and nitra.
- Golden Lootbugs are larger and shinier than regular lootbugs, and upon death drop a rather generous amount of gold. We're rich!
- Huuli Hoarders are uncommon passive creatures, which quickly run away when attacked. Upon death, they will drop some gold and nitra, as well as a loot crate. The loot crate can contain gold, nitra, as well as upgrades and even rare artifacts.

The "Hurricane" Guided Rocket System is one of the weapons available to the Gunner and Engineer in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching class rank 21 on the Gunner.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Demolitionist] Demolitionist class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Hurricane is a weapon where its strength relies in its simplicity. Upon firing, a volley of 9 rockets are sent flying into the sky, before falling back down into randomly targetted bugs. If no bugs are found, they will simply fall aimlessly into the ground. It will continue to unload its entire clip whenever it finishes reloading, regardless of bug presence. Because of this, terrain obstruction is a non-issue with this weapon, as the rockets will simply fly over it. Its pool of upgrades is also very small, with its only options being either reload speed, or pure damage. One thing to note would be that, the Hurricane does not consider itself to have any ammo capacity, this means the weapon begins reloading immediately upon firing the first rocket of its "clip", and has effectively no delay between each reload.
The only downside to note about it would be that, since it is classified as a [HEAVY] weapon, the Hurricane suffers from a very slow reloading speed of 6 seconds. However, due to the lack of delay between each reload, and its already small upgrade pool, this downside is relatively negligible, and can be quickly remedied after a few level ups.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the "Hurricane" Guided Rocket System Weapon Mastery in Crystalline Caverns on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Explosion Radius

## Notable Quirks
- The Incendiary Payload overclock adds the [GROUNDZONE] tag to the weapon. Rockets leave fiery groundzones after they explode. While the fire on the ground from Incendiary Payload does deal [FIRE] damage and apply the Burn status effect, the Hurricane does not receive the [FIRE] tag and it cannot receive potency upgrades, meaning that there is little value in trying to build Hurricane for status damage. However, Incendiary Payload is a very strong OC because the damage it deals is based off of the damage from the rockets themselves.
Incendiary Payload Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 18 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.1 (rounded up)
| Lifetime | 12s | 
| Radius | 1.5 units | 
| Potency | 10 Burn | Base Potency * 0.5
- Reload Speed affects damage over time as the weapon will immediately launch rockets after reloading and will not wait until it finishes launching a set of rockets before launching the next set. This makes stacking reload speed a powerful tool when upgrading your Hurricane.

| | Let me have that hurricane!
| | Rocket launcher, baby!
| | I'll guide those rockets right to the bugs!
| | I'm feeling like launching rockets!
| | It's a hurricane of hurt, baby!

## Notable Quirks
## EM Discharge
The Stubby will attempt to target friendly targets within range. Friendly targets that are hit by the Stubby's projectiles cause a 3 range radius explosion, and go into cooldown for 0.75 seconds before they can be hit or targeted again. The damage dealt by the explosion is the same as the weapon's damage, and it applies the weapon's full potency. Friendly targets can still trigger the explosion even if they are not being actively targeted by the Stubby.

| “ | Shoots in front of you in a steady rhythm. Knocks back enemiesShoots in move directionDeals [KINETIC] damage
| — Weapon description
The "Warthog" Auto 210 is one of the weapons available to the Engineer by default in DRG Survivor. It is the starting weapon for the [Class Mod Tinkerer] Tinkerer class mod.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The "Warthog" Auto 210's behavior differs from other weapons by the fact that it is one of the very few to which the survivor has some form of control over its aiming. Its firing angle is completely dependant on the direction the survivor is facing, rather than the presence and proximity of bugs around them, and will keep firing on its own no matter if there are bugs to shoot or not. Its rate of fire, uniquely enough, is in perfect synchronisation with its reloading speed, effectively allowing it to keep shooting with great consistency. Its damage per shot is also incredibly high, and can be considered as one of the highest in the entire game. Do note that the weapon's base damage is not per shot, but per pellet, meaning that if one wants to know the true amount of damage per shot, they must multiply the total damage by the amount of pellets per shot. Combined with the knockback caused by the pellets, the high piercing potential, and the tight spread of the shots, makes the "Warthog" Auto 210 a formidable weapon all around.
The only major downside this weapon has however is, as mentioned earlier, the absolute dependency on the survivor's facing direction. It is completely unable to aim on its own, and requires the survivor to guide its shots. In cases where they need to move away from the bugs for example, the survivor must turn around to land their shots on the pursuing bugs, eventually relying on hit-and-run tactics.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the "Warthog" Auto 210 Weapon Mastery in Salt Pits on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

The "Thunderhead" Heavy Autocannon is one of the weapons available to the Gunner in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 12 on the Gunner, and it is the starting weapon for the [Class Mod Heavy Gunner] Heavy Gunner class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
Unique to this weapon, the Autocannon fires a volley of bullets which upon firing the first shot, will lock the angle at which it has been fired for the rest of the clip, and continue firing until it is empty. Due to the fact that it prioritizes shooting at bugs with the most health, and because of the locked firing angle, this allows the survivor to easily pick their targets by repositioning themselves while the weapon is unloading its clip. Coupled with its massive damage per shot and large clip size, makes it a devastating weapon for single targets. Its bullets also have the potential of slightly knocking away bugs, allowing it to slow them down, or even prevent them from approaching, depending on the upgrades to fire rate.
However, the Autocannon has a painstakingly long reloading time, which can put the survivor in a vulnerable state far longer than most other weapons. Coupled with the locked angle upon firing, if shot in the wrong direction, can potentially waste the entire clip without proper repositioning, costing another long-lasting reload in the process. The knockback caused by its bullets can also knock the bugs sideway, making aiming with the weapon slightly more inconsistent overall, notably on smaller bugs.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the "Thunderhead" Heavy Autocannon Weapon Mastery in Azure Weald on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

# Acid  (alias/duplicados: ACID damage)
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsDamageAcid
Other types of damage include [KINETIC], [ELECTRICAL], [COLD], [FIRE] and [PLASMA]
[ACID] is a damage type in DRG Survivor.
[ACID] damage applies Corrode to the target, with the number of stacks equal to an elemental weapon's Potency. Corrode deals moderate damage, 0.5 dmg/stack/tick. It also increases the damage the target takes by 0.25% per stack up to a max of 100% at 400 stacks. When an affected creature dies, it applies a small number of Corrode stacks to nearby hostiles.

# Anomaly Dives
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDivesAnomaly Dives
Anomaly Dives menu
Anomaly Dives are dives with special challenges. They are unlocked by earning Mastery Points ([Mastery Point]). Completing a dive awards Anomaly Points ([icon Anomaly]).
## Dives
## [Notice Bubble] Bloody Learner
| “ | Some dwarves insist that "learning things the hard way" is, in fact, the best way to do it.
| — Bloody Learner description
The Bloody Learner dive is unlocked at [Mastery Point] 5. It has [icon Anomaly] 100 available.
Rules:
- You take damage whenever you level up
- +100% XP gain
- Start with: BLT Ration Pack
- Unavailable artifacts: Vita-Miner Pills
## [Notice Bubble] Company Man
| “ | Management asks that you focus on fulfilling your quotas for these dives. Remember why you're here, miner.
| — Company Man description
The Company Man dive is unlocked at [Mastery Point] 10. It has [icon Anomaly] 100 available.
Rules:
- You gain no XP from killing enemies.
- +300% XP from mining
- +300% XP from objectives
- Extra secondary objective
## [Notice Bubble] Mutator Madness
| “ | Hold on to your beards for these dives: scans show a particularly high density of anomalies in this region.
| — Mutator Madness description
The Mutator Madness dive is unlocked at [Mastery Point] 15. It has [icon Anomaly] 100 available. The dive will have a handful of Mutators applied, or random changes to the gamerules. The player is given a choice of Mutator cards at the beginning of each stage. The first set is permanent and lasts the whole dive, but every subsequent choice of Mutators only lasts the one stage.
Rules:
- Add Mutators at the end of every stage
Hazard level affects the number of permanent (stage one) Mutators:
[Hazard 1] Hazard 1: 1 positive, 1 negative
[Hazard 2] Hazard 2: 1 positive, 2 negative
[Hazard 3] Hazard 3: 1 positive, 2 negative
[Hazard 4] Hazard 4: 1 positive, 2 negative
[Hazard 5] Hazard 5: 1 positive, 2 negative
## [Notice Bubble] No Movement
| “ | Whether this began as a prank, a drunken dare, or some kind of test of will, no dwarf remembers - but the tradition continues to this day.
| — No Movement description
The No Movement dive is unlocked at [Mastery Point] 25. It has [icon Anomaly] 40 available.
Rules:
- Can only move for the first 5 sec after the drop pod has arrived
## [Notice Bubble] Hardcore
| “ | Only footwork can save you here, miner. Footwork, and a lot of sweat.
| — Hardcore description
The Hardcore dive is unlocked at [Mastery Point] 20. It has [icon Anomaly] 100 available.
Rules:
- -90% to Max HP and all Max HP gains
- Unavailable artifacts: Energy Bars
## [Notice Bubble] Vanilla
| “ | After your latest bender, management has decided to demote you. It's back to basics with these dives!
| — Vanilla description
The Vanilla dive is unlocked at [Mastery Point] 30. It has [icon Anomaly] 60 available.
Rules:
- No Gear
- No Meta upgrades
- No Mastery Bonus

## Hazard
Hazard levels are basic difficulties chosen from the outset of most mission types. They range from Haz 1 to Haz 5. Greater Hazards offer more Resources and higher-level Gear, but the bugs are stronger.
## Biome & Stage Difficulty
## Alien Threat Level
| “ | The aliens grow stronger!
| 
Alien Threat Level increases when the player is dilly dallying. During Elimination missions, threat level increases by one every 60 seconds during boss battles. During Escort Duty, threat level increases periodically throughout the mission.
## Mutators
Main: Survivor:Mutators
Mutators add special gamerules to a mission, like "Increases shop prices by 50%" or "Weapons jam sometimes."

| “ | Defensive drones that damage and slow enemies on contactDeals [COLD] damage
| — Weapon description
The Arc-Tek Cryo Guard is one of the weapons available to the Scout in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 15 on the Scout.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker and Demolisher's [Class Mod Operator] Operator class mods.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Cryo Guard receives damage, potency, reload speed, and drone count upgrades, but it will also receive tag masteries for other weapons that share the [COLD], [CONSTRUCT], [LASTING], and [DRONE] tags with it.
The player begins with 4 drones that circle clockwise around the player at a distance of 4 units at 90° degrees a second, applying 90 [COLD] damage and applying 24 stacks of Slow to whatever enemy they touch in their path. Each drone effectively completes its orbit around the player every 4 seconds. The Cryo Guard deals the highest base damage of all drone weapons.
The Behaviour Chip: Aggro Overclock changes the drones' behavior. They will rotate around the player as normal until an enemy appears within range, after which they will attack the enemy or groups of enemies in a circular motion. Should the player wander too far from the drones, they will try to return to the player and resume circling. 
The Crisis Protocol overclock changes the drones' behavior, sending them into a frantic frenzy upon the player taking damage. For 2.5 seconds, the drones will rush to and swarm around their owner in a criss cross formation at 1.5x their normal movement speed, after which they will resume either Aggro or normal behavior, depending if the Aggro OC is present.
The Disposable Tech Overclock adds the [EXPLOSIVE] and [GROUNDZONE] tags and causes the drones to explode at the end of their lifetime which knock back enemies and leave an invisible ground zone with the following stats:
Disposable Tech Explosion Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 360 | Base Damage * 4
| Radius | 3 units | 
Disposable Tech Explosion Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 9 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.1
| Lifetime | 2s | 
| Radius | 3 units | 
| Potency | 8 Slow | Base Potency / 3
Disposable Tech also reverses the effect of lifetime upgrades, making them shorten the lifetime of the drone instead of lengthening it and resulting in more explosions and more ground zones. Additionally, lifetime upgrades will increase the duration of the ground zones, making lifetime a very strong stat if you are running Disposable Tech on your drones. 
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Arc-Tek Cryo Guard Weapon Mastery in Azure Weald on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Potency
## Strategy
- Early-game, the Cryo Guard is best used as a support weapon to keep ample space between you and the bugs by freezing whatever is within their attack radius while your other weapons do the work. One must take caution in the early game and keep this fact in mind: cryo drones lack the status effect damage and aggressive behavior that make the Viper and the Firefly so devastating to small groups, even without upgrades. They struggle to output decent damage early-game and rely on damage and potency upgrades to freeze and kill bugs.
- To maximize its damage and freezing efficacy, build the Cryo Guard with the following upgrades in mind, assuming all upgrades (Except +1 Drone, which is always legendary and should always be taken) are the same rarity:
- +1 Drone > Damage > Reload Speed > Potency Reload Speed is not much of a priority early game because the drones already have a long lifetime and the cryo drones already have enough potency to get the job done. However, taking epic and legendary Potency and Reload Speed upgrades if they're the highest rarity cards available is always the best option.
- After leveling your Cryo Guard, consider building around these overclocks, listed in priority:
- Disposable Tech is hands down the best OC for the Cryo Guard. Each drone explodes for 4x base damage in a 3m zone, and with lifetime tag mastery upgrades and decreasing the periods between drone uptime and drone explosions, the drones will deal constant, devastating damage.
- Personal Space Invaders is next in OC priority, especially if Disposable Tech had already been taken. Having two more drones 50% closer to the player provides ample protection, and, with enough potency, the player can run through small groups of enemies with impunity... And explode for massive damage near large targets with D-Tech. In addition, circling counterclockwise around groups of enemies increases the chance that the closely-packed drones strike for damage and apply freeze--and, again, explode with D-Tech.
- Behavior Chip: Aggro synergizes well with both Disposable Tech and Personal Space Invaders. Taking this with D-Tech ensures all drones explode near their targets (or the unfortunate single target), while pairing it with PSI negates the need for the circling-counterclockwise strategy, as the drones will ravage and freeze anything near the player.
- More Drones... Adds another drone. With the myriad sources of extra drones, this OC is best left in the dust.
- When choosing Unstable Overclocks...
- Avoid Crisis Protocol. The drones do EVERYTHING but rush to protect you. Karl forbids a grunt nip at your heel; the drones will haphazardly fly around the screen in a broad crisscross formation, far away from you, and far away from the bug that attacked you. With enough weapon range, they will haphazardly fly to the edges of the screen, or even go off-screen. During a Weapon Mastery, they literally leave you at the mercy of the bugs closest to you. Never take this. Ever.
- Take More Drones instead. Always. More drones, more damage, more stacks, more explosions, more goodness.

The ArmsKore Coil Gun is one of the weapons available to Gunner in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching class rank 27 on the Gunner.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The gun shoots a beam with a width of 1m and a range of 10m that does 80 initial damage and 16 damage every half second for three seconds. Should the gun obtain additional beams, each beam shoots at a 15° angle away from the center beam. The weapon does not require a line of sight to fire.
The Explosive Reload OC adds the [EXPLOSIVE] tag to the weapon, giving it an extreme amount of knockback and making it deal 80 base damage in a 7 unit radius around the player every time the weapon reloads.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the ArmsKore Coil Gun Weapon Mastery in Salt Pits on Hazard 4+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

# Artifacts  (alias/duplicados: Supply Pod)
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentArtifacts
Artifacts are available during dives from the Supply Pod event.
## Supply Pod
[Supply] 
| “ | Supply pod prepped. Clear out the landing zone and triangulate your position, so it can land.
| — Mission Control
During each stage in a dive, Mission Control prepares a Supply Pod for the player. A landing zone will be marked on the minimap and an circular zone is drawn around the supply beacon, indicating the area that must be cleared of obstacles before the supply pod can land.
Opening the pod presents a selection of three artifacts drawn from those the player has unlocked. If the player has purchased artifact re-rolls in the meta upgrades menu, they can spend Nitra to re-roll the selection.
The pod’s impact deals heavy damage to enemies. On lower hazard levels, this can kill an elite from full health. However, on higher hazard levels the damage it deals to elites is reduced. A contextual voice line plays when an enemy is killed by the drop

# Azure Weald  (alias/duplicados: Biome, Biomes, Crystalline Caverns, Glacial Strata, Hollow Bough, Magma Core, Main Missions, Salt Pits)
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDivesBiomes
[Campaign Illustration]
There are currently 5 biomes available in DRG Survivor: Crystalline Caverns, Magma Core, Hollow Bough, Salt Pits, and Azure Weald. Each biome has 5 different Hazard levels that must be unlocked with Biome Points. These can be earned by completing the unique challenges that are shown when choosing the Hazard level.
## Crystalline Caverns
| “ | Although many greenbeards begin their career here, don't be fooled by the beauty of this place. The Jewel of Hoxxes is as dangerous as it is breathtaking.
| — Crystalline Caverns description
The Crystalline Caverns are unlocked by default. This is a standard biome with purple mineable terrain and purple crystalline structures.
Enemies are at the default difficulty.

## Magma Core
| “ | When you're this close to the core of Hoxxes, angry glyphids are the least of your worries. Between the hot rocks, exploding plants, and magma flows, you'll be lucky to extract without a singed beard!
| — Magma Core description
Magma Core is a step up in difficulty from the Crystalline Caverns. It features dark rocky mineable terrain, lava vents, and exploding plants. Cracked ground and terrain with exposed lava deals damage to miners who come into contact. Exploding plants trigger when the player comes too close.
Enemies have 5% more HP and deal 5% more damage.

## Hollow Bough
| “ | A strange maze of regrowing vines and thorns that is a challenge to navigate for even the most experienced graybeards. If you want to make it to extraction you'd best keep digging.
| — Hollow Bough description
The Hollow Bough introduces another new mechanic for a new challenge. It features green mineable terrain, thin brown vines, and thick red vines. The brown vines are regenerating vines, meaning they will reappear a short time after mining. This is important for the Hazard 1 challenge shown below. The thick red vines are able to be mined but will damage the player if they contact them and will also regenerate after a short time.
Enemies have 10% more HP and deal 10% more damage.

## Salt Pits
| “ | A lovely place where mining is a breeze and the rocks are tasty. If it weren't for the unstable stalactites and those pesky Q'ronar you might like it here.
| — Salt Pits description
The Salt Pits feature white rocky terrain on a red dirt background. Most of the terrain in the Salt Pits is easier to mine compared to the stone in other biomes, except for dark gray strips which have a normal break speed. A new mechanic is added with this biome. Players will come across large red pulsating crystal clusters in the ground that, upon mining, will collapse several unstable stalactites from the ceiling that can kill monsters.
Enemies have 15% more HP and deal 15% more damage.

## Azure Weald
| “ | Filled with brilliant bioluminescent light, the Weald can seem almost peaceful at times. However, to the greybeard it's clear that the law of the jungle still applies here. When the glyphids finally arrive, they are as fierce as ever.
| — Azure Weald description
The Azure Weald is a wet and lush area featuring trees, jump pads and healing crystals. Walking across a jump pad launches a dwarf high in the air, and when they crash back down they destroy terrain under them, push bugs away, and are granted a haste boost for a few seconds. There is no Red Sugar; instead, circles of crystalline columns offer healing over time when the player (and any creature) comes near.
Several new species of glyphid occur here, including Wardens, Mini Exploders (who replace Exploders) and Big Praetorians. Enemy spawn rates differ; instead of a steady background trickle of bugs, Azure Weald will have periods of relative quiet followed by heavy spawn rates. Enemies have [?]% more HP and deal [?]% more damage.
[Hex Azure Weald]

## Glacial Strata
| “ | Maintaining your footing is perhaps the toughest task in this frozen biome. The extreme cold has forced the glyphids to adapt in unusual ways, as should you if you hope to make it back.
| — Glacial Strata description
Glacial Strata is a frozen cave system featuring green icicles and patches of smooth ice. The smooth ice increases the speed of all dwarfs sliding across it at the cost of control. 
Enemies have ?% more HP and deal ?% more damage.

| “ | Shoots rapidly in four directionsDeals [KINETIC] damage
| — Weapon description
The BRT7 Burst Fire Gun is one of the weapons available to Gunner in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 3 on the Gunner.
It constantly shoots in four directions at once, parallel to the world grid and at a 65 degree angle offset clockwise. The stock weapon can deal up to 93 DPS, per direction, per bug pierced (adjusted for reload downtime).
It can also be equipped while using Scout's [Class Mod Recon] Recon class mod.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the BRT7 Burst Fire Gun Weapon Mastery in Hollow Bough on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

# Bosco
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentBosco
APD-B317, known as 'Bosco'
🚧 PARDON OUR DUST -- This page is under construction 🚧
Bosco is a hovering, All-Purpose Drone that automatically follows you during dives. Unlike the more command-heavy version in Deep Rock Galactic, Bosco behaves mostly autonomously as a combat helper.
## Behaviour
Bosco floats near you, and automatically targets nearby enemies and fires at them, functioning mostly as a defensive drone.
Bosco's default weapon shoots a burst of [KINETIC] projectiles, similar to the Deepcore GK2.
## Modifications
Bosco can be modified through the use of Chip gear pieces, which in addition to granting the player stat boosts, they change how his weapon behaves or improves its effectiveness (in Rare, Epic or Legendary rarities).

| “ | Shoots bouncing projectiles connected by a beam of plasmaTargets groups of enemiesDeals [PLASMA] damage
| — Weapon description
The Breach Cutter is one of the weapons available to the Engineer in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 15 on the Engineer.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Heavy Gunner] Heavy Gunner class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The gun shoots two balls of ricocheting plasma connected to each other by a beam, visualized as such:
O—O
As the balls move away from each other, the beam grows longer. Should the weapon accrue more beams, each additional beam count adds a ball with its own connection, visualized as such:
O—O—O
Each ball is separated by another ball by twenty degrees; each ball and beam have a lifetime of 5 seconds, during which they infinitely pierce enemies at 35 damage every 0.2 seconds. 
The Experimental Cluster Projectiles overclock splits each plasma ball into 4 balls at the end of the beam's lifetime. Each ball scatters from the other at 150% speed while retaining the same damage and lifetime.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Breach Cutter Weapon Mastery in Salt Pits on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Reload Speed
## Overclocks
| Icon | Name | Effects | Game description
| [OC Balanced][Reload Damage] | A Little More Oomph! | +25% Damage+25% Reload Speed | Increases damage and reload speed
| [OC Balanced][Damage Fire] | Hot Plasma | +15% Damage+X Burn | Superheats the plamsa, burning enemies
| [OC Balanced][Damage Electrical] | Ionized Plasma | +15% Damage+<1.1 Potency> Shock | Ionizes the plasma, shocking enemies
| [OC Balanced][Beam Count] | More Beams | +1 Beams | Adds more beams
| [OC Unstable][More Beams] | Even More Beams | +3 Beams | That's a lot of beams
| [OC Unstable][Explode Despawn] | Experimental Cluster Projectiles | — | Plasma balls split into smaller projectiles when they expire
## Voice Lines
| | Breaches, bugs... This'll cut anything!
| | This'll cut a bug in two!
| | I want the big, scary Breach Cutter.
| | Breach Cutter: like a hot knife through butter.
| | Nothing better than the Breach Cutter.
| | That Breach Cutter's calling my name.

# COLD damage
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsDamageCold
Other types of damage include [KINETIC], [ELECTRICAL], [FIRE], [ACID] and [PLASMA]
[COLD] is a damage type in DRG Survivor.
[COLD] damage applies Slow to the target, with the number of stacks applied affected by an elemental weapon's Potency. Slow slows down the target and has a chance to apply Frozen every time more stacks are added. Slowness is modified by the target's slowness resistance. Frozen stops the target completely for several seconds. The chance of application and duration are modified by the target's freeze resistance.

The CRSPR Flamethower is one of the weapons available to Driller by default in DRG Survivor. It is the starting weapon of the [Class Mod Interrogator] Interrogator class mod.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Heavy Gunner] Heavy Gunner class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The CRSPR shoots 2 beams - one in front and one behind the player - at a range of 5 units, rotating counterclockwise at a speed of 35° per second, for 12 seconds. Whenever the beam makes contact with an enemy, it deals 40 damage every 0.2 seconds and applies 12 stacks of Burn damage every 0.5 seconds. 
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the CRSPR Flamethrower Weapon Mastery in Crystalline Caverns on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Weapon Range

The Chimera Fragcannon is one of the weapons available to Demolisher by default in DRG Survivor.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist and Gunner's [Class Mod Heavy Gunner] Heavy Gunner class mods.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Chimera Fragcannon delivers a considerable amount of damage.
- There are 3 different sets of Unstable Overclocks that can be offered, depending on whether you took the Electrified Rounds or Plasmatic Rounds balanced overclocks, or neither.
- The Volt Splitter unstable overclock allows the weapon to shoot 5 projectiles.
- The overclocks Electrified Rounds adds the [ELECTRICAL] tag, Plasmatic Rounds adds the [PLASMA] tag, and Hot Plasma adds the [FIRE] tag.
- Hot Plasma and Ionized Plasma unstable overclocks require the Plasmatic Rounds balanced overclock to be offered.
- Similarly, Lead Wrapped Ammo and Volt Splitter unstable overclocks require the Electrified Rounds balanced overclock to be offered.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Chimera Fragcannon Weapon Mastery in Hollow Bough on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage
