# Classic  (alias/duplicados: Recon, Scout, Sharp Shooter)
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesScout
| “ | The most nimble of dwarves, the Scout relies on long range weapons and increased mobility to faster explore and escape the dangers of Hoxxes IV
| — Class description
The Scout is one of five playable classes in DRG Survivor, being unlocked by default and the only character available in the game's demo.
The Scout class
Scout specializes in mobility and evasion, dodging around enemies while his weapons passively do damage. Scout also has a higher base Critical Damage multiplier than the other classes. His starting weapons consist of the Deepcore GK2, Jury-Rigged Boomstick, Zhukov NUK17, and Cryo Grenade. His class mods are Classic, Recon, and Sharp Shooter, with Classic being unlocked by default.
## Stats
The Scout Class's baseline stats are below. Stats may be altered by upgrades or by choosing a Class Mod.
Starting Stats
| Health | 120
| Dodge Chance | 5%
| Critical Chance | 5%
| Critical Damage | 200%
## Available Weapons
Scout has a set of ten weapons that, once unlocked by ranking up in the class, can always be found by him during regular dives.
The Recon Scout also has access to all unlocked [LIGHT] weapons.

| “ | A hand-held microwave emitter that splits its damage between all enemies caught in its coneDeals [FIRE] damage
| — Weapon description
The Colette Wave Cooker is one of the weapons available to the Driller in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Class Rank 6 on the Driller.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
As a [BEAM] weapon, the Colette Wave Cooker is capable of firing a wide cone towards groups of bugs, hitting them with direct [FIRE] damage and applying 24 stacks of Burn per second. The weapon's cone rotates slowly when tracking its targets, but immediately sets its angle to the closest bug after a reload. This weapon is capable of piercing an unlimited number of bugs inside its cone, and it can fire through terrain without penalty. The most notable strength of this weapon comes from its high potency combined with its wide beam, allowing it to set large groups of bugs on fire for high DoT potential. Because of this, it is the perfect weapon for the Interrogator Class Mod, who already grants doubled effects on DoT as a whole, but other classes can still gain great benefits from this weapon.
Careful considerations include the Wave Cooker's slow reloading speed and rotation speed. The weapon struggles at picking targets, so its slow reaction time may force the survivor to reposition themselves in order to help the weapon hit its targets. This can be improved with the Better Ball Bearings Overclock, which doubles its rotation speed and gives the weapon more reactivity.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Colette Wave Cooker Weapon Mastery in Hollow Bough on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Potency
## Overclocks
| Icon | Name | Effects | Game description
| [OC Balanced][Fast Spin] | Better Ball Bearings | — | Increase rotation speed
| [OC Balanced][Beam Spread] | Crowd Cooker | — | Increase beam width
| [OC Balanced][Beam Range] | Focused Lens | +200% Damage | Increase beam length but reduce width. Target the enemy with the largest total HP
| [OC Balanced][Nano Waves] | Nano Waves | +100% Potency-30% Damage | Less initial damage, but increases the applied burn stacks
| [OC Unstable][Central Deflector] | Centralized Reflector | +10% Potency | Adds an additional beam that fires in the opposite direction
| [OC Unstable][Damage Cryo] | Thermal Oscillator | +25% Damage+<30% Potency> Slow | WaveCooker now also applies Slow
## Overclock Data explained further
- Better Ball Bearings increases the rotation speed of the beam by a factor of 2.
- Crowd Cooker increases beam width by 30%.
- Focused lens increases the beam's range by 3 units but decreases the beam angle by 40%.

# Contractor  (alias/duplicados: Demolisher, Gridrunner, Operator)
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesDemolisher
| “ | The Demolisher is all about bringing heavy machinery to the caverns below. Using his trusty Rockdozer, he ploughs through any obstacle in his path and leaves a trail of destruction in his wake.
| — Class description
The Demolisher is one of five playable classes in DRG Survivor, being unlocked by purchasing the Heavy Duty Expansion DLC.
The Demolisher class
## Controls
Unlike traditional classes that fight on foot, the Demolisher operates from within the Rockdozer, a heavily armed industrial vehicle that trades traditional mobility for overwhelming destructive power and terrain manipulation.
The Demolisher offers two distinct control schemes that players can toggle in the settings to suit their preference:
- Standard (Tank Controls)
- For steering, moving the joystick Left/Right, or using A/D rotates the vehicle on its axis.
- Throttling (Joystick Up or W key) and reversing (Joystick Down or S key) moves the vehicle in the direction it is currently facing.
- Allows for precise movement and aiming for rear-mounted weapons.
- Simplified (Directional Controls)
- The vehicle automatically turns and moves toward whichever direction you hold on the joystick or keyboard.
- If you pull the stick directly opposite of your current facing direction, the vehicle will perform a tight U-turn or shift into reverse automatically depending on your speed.
- Best for beginners, as the steering is handled automatically, letting you focus on dodging enemies.
## Stats
The Demolisher Class's baseline stats are below. Stats may be altered by upgrades or by choosing a Class Mod.
Starting Stats
| Health | 175
| Armor | 10
| Critical Chance | 5%
| Critical Damage | 150%
## Available Weapons
Demolisher has a set of eleven weapons that, once unlocked by ranking up in the class, can always be found by him during regular dives.
The Gridrunner Demolisher also has access to all unlocked [ELECTRIC] weapons, and the Operator Demolisher has access to all [DRONE] weapons.

# Credits
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
Breadcrumbs Cargo Template Error: 
- Page "Survivor:Credits" is not in Database
| [Drg stub] | This article is a stub. You can help Deep Rock Galactic Wiki by expanding it.
This article is a stub. You can help Deep Rock Galactic Wiki by expanding it.
Credits are used to purchase permanent Upgrades from the main menu. 
All resources collected during a dive award Credits at the end, including Gold, Nitra, and Upgrade Resources. Credit payments are based on what is picked up, not the remaining amount of resources at the end of the dive, so Gold and Nitra can be freely spent on upgrades.
Morkite also earns Credits, even after completing the objective to gather it, but Boolo Caps and Apoca Blooms do not. According to a developer, this is an oversight as of version 0.2.146d.
In addition to collecting resources, Credits are awarded for every bug killed. All enemy types pay the same amount.
Every character level reached during a dive awards Credits.
Higher hazard levels pay more Credits, and progressing further in a dive awards more Credits, including a bonus for successfully completing it.

 SurvivorDwarvesScoutEquipmentCryo Gren.

| “ | A grenade that deals moderate damage and slows enemiesDeals [COLD] damage
| — Weapon description
The Cryo Grenade is one of the weapons available to Scout by default in DRG Survivor.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Demolitionist] Demolitionist and Driller's [Class Mod Strong Armed] Strong Armed class mods.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The player throws the grenade, which has an air time of 1.5 seconds before detonating. The detonation deals 100 [COLD] damage and adds 60 stacks of Slow to whatever is in its 4 unit explosion radius. An enemy does not need to be within a the player's to toss the the grenade.
The Overclock, The Favourite, adds [THE FAVOURITE] tag.
## Strategy
- The Cryo Grenade is a beautifully-refined weapon with two simple tasks: deal massive damage and freeze bugs. It accomplishes these two tasks with aplomb with no investment; it boasts the highest base potency of any [COLD] weapon without overclocks at 60 Slow stacks, capable of freezing the chunkiest of Praetorians and mini elites, and 100 damage per anything within its 4 unit explosion radius is nothing to sneeze at.
- The Cryo Grenade does not have a fire rate stat or a card for it; its reload speed IS its fire rate!
- The grenade only has three upgrade cards, which should be prioritized as follows, assuming all cards are the same rarity:
- - Reload Speed = Damage > Potency
- Reload Speed and Damage upgrades should be taken as a 'highest card rarity' and 'as needed' regimen. Despite the chilly nade's impressive stats, its reload time suffers at a staggering 5 seconds for a single grenade. To offset this weakness, prioritize reload speed early-game; then, after you're spamming grenades left and right, boost its damage mid and late-game.
- Potency upgrades should only be taken if they're the highest rarity available.
- As the player levels their Cryo Grenade, they will unlock Overclocks, each tailored for specific builds. The following overclocks are listed in priority of causing the most destruction to the bugs and keeping the player safe:
- Cluster Grenades are the bread and butter of the chilly nades. With this overclock, instead of one grenade, the player tosses 3 smaller grenades, each one at 80% damage. Even with the 20% damage, potency, and explosion radius reduction penalties, the overclock is a net 192% damage gain, especially if all those grenades hit the same group of enemies.
- A Little More Oomph! is the supplementary overclock to go with Cluster Grenades. +25% damage and +25% reload speed equates to a +50% net gain of damage over time; again, its reload speed is its fire rate.
- Compact Explosives, if the occasional unlucky dwarf does not receive clusters with his first overclock, is their best option if they took ALMO first. With enough explosion radius tag mastery upgrades, one can freeze the entire screen, and then some.
- True TNT is a situational overclock tailored towards Demolitionist Engie and Strong Armed Driller, or if one loves to destroy terrain. Otherwise, there are better overclocks for the Cryo Grenade.
- After leveling the Cryo Grenade to level 18, the player can choose two Unstable Overclocks:
- Gravitational Core increases the explosion radius of the grenade by +25%. While it does not give much utility to a Cluster build, it synergizes well with a Compact Explosives and True TNT-based build, increasing the explosion radius by a total of 55% without Cluster Rounds, and, with enough investment, can clear entire screens of bugs and rocks. Be wary, however, that the grenade pulls in enemies before detonation. Staying too close to a grenade before it detonates could drag some bugs right on top of a hapless dwarf's hitbox.
- - The Favourite is an excellent UOC choice for those who wish to freeze spam a horde of incoming elites and their big Dreadnought brethren all with the same grenade, and destroy them almost as quickly. This variant of The Favourite is unique in that it does not decrease other weapons' damage, so it is best taken into consideration with other weapons that do not rely as much on reload speed or potency.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Cryo Grenade Weapon Mastery in Crystalline Caverns on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage
## Overclocks

# Damage
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsDamage
[Status Effect Thumbnail]
Each of the weapons provided by Deep Rock Galactic, come with their own types of elemental damage, giving the dwarves plenty of ways for them to kill bugs.
Some weapons, with the help of Overclocks, may also be given the option to switch their element into a different one, or be given an element on top of their existing one. For example, one may want to switch their weapon's [KINETIC] damage into a [FIRE] damage, allowing them to apply strong status effect damage to bugs.
Mid-dive upgrades exclusive to a specific element, will apply to all weapons of a Survivor's kit, so long as they are of that element. Should the element of their weapons be switched, any upgrade bonuses relative to these elements won't be provided if the weapon's element no longer matches, or will if it does, and vice-versa.
## Damage Types
[Kinetic Damage] [KINETIC] – basic non-elemental damage
[Damage Fire] [FIRE] – inflicts Burn on the target, which deals heavy damage over time
[Damage Electrical] [ELECTRICAL] – inflicts Shock on the target, increasing critical hit chance against them and dealing damage to nearby enemies
[Damage Acid] [ACID] – inflicts Corrode on the target, which increases damage taken from all sources including itself
[Damage Cryo] [COLD] – inflicts Slow on the target, which decreases move speed and has a stacking chance to Freeze the target
[Damage Plasma] [PLASMA] – shots bounce off targets and terrain
## Ground Zones
Some weapons will leave a lingering effect on the ground - the ticking damage from this effect is usually equal to a percentage of the damage of a hit from the weapon itself, which is extremely strong for weapons that deal a lot of damage in one hit (such as Impact Axe or Experimental Plasma Charger).
Most ground zones will also apply status effect stacks as a bonus, equal to 50% of the weapon's Potency - this is extremely strong for any weapon if you are building for status effect damage, described below.
## Status Effect Damage
Elemental weapons ([FIRE] [ACID] [ELECTRICAL] or [COLD]), and also weapons that have been granted status effects via Overclocks, apply Status Effect Stacks when they hit enemies. Stacks apply effects and/or deal damage, and decay by 20% every tick (0.5 sec).
- Burn deals strong damage (1 dmg/stack/tick).
- Shock deals moderate damage (0.5 dmg/stack/tick) when other enemies are nearby, increases crit chance against targets (0.125% Crit Chance per stack, up to a max of 100%), and arcs to nearby targets, applying a small number of stacks.
- Corrode deals moderate damage (0.5 dmg/stack/tick), increases the damage the target takes from all sources including itself (.25% per stack, up to a max of 100%), and applies a small number of stacks to nearby targets if the target dies
- Slow slows down the targets and has a chance to inflict Frozen on hit; slowness modified by target's freeze resistance.
- Frozen stops the target completely for several seconds; chance of application and duration modified by target's freeze resistance.
The more stacks you apply, the stronger these effects are. Damage dealt by these stacks (known as Status Effect Damage) benefits from any and all damage increases, with a couple of exceptions:
- It is not increased by Bigger Cogs upgrades, except for the Bigger Cogs upgrade for the matching element ([FIRE] [ACID] or [ELECTRICAL])
- It cannot crit, so it is unaffected by Critical Chance and Critical Damage upgrades
Potency is how many Status Effect Stacks are applied per direct hit. More potency means your status effects are stronger:
- Fire – increased ticking damage
- Electrical – increased crit chance and ticking damage
- Acid – increased damage amp and ticking damage
- Cold – freeze bugs faster
Status Effect Damage and Potency are multiplicative with each other.
## How Shock Status Effect Works
## Shock Stacks
The first step is to deal [ELECTRICAL] damage to an enemy. When you deal [ELECTRICAL] on-hit damage, your weapon will apply stacks of Shock per hit. The number of stacks applied per hit is equal to the potency as shown on the weapon inspect screen.
## Shock Tick
Status effects tick every 0.5 seconds, dealing damage based on the stack count and your various damage modifiers, and losing 20% of the stacks. Unlike the other damage dealing status effects (Burn and Corrode) which deal their damage to the bug under the status effect, Shock does not deal damage to the bug under the status effect. Shocked bugs deal damage to other nearby bugs.
For Shock to tick, there must be a nearby bug within a small range. If there are no nearby bugs, Shock will not tick and the Shocked bug will not lose any stacks. When Shock ticks, the Shocked bug damages a nearby bug based on the stack count, and then the Shocked bug loses stacks (Burn and Corrode lose stacks before ticking). When the tick occurs, you will see an very subtle arc of lightning between the Shocked bug and the nearby bug.
## Shock Spread
Shock is unique amongst the status effects, where with each tick (where the status effect doesn't fall off), it spreads to the bug that was damaged by the Shock. Each spreading occurrence is always for 8 stacks.
## Rinse and Repeat
This whole process repeats while bugs are in range and while the bugs have enough stacks. Either you continue to apply stacks or the stacks decay until they fall off completely.
## Strategy
- Due to the different effects provided by each type of element, one can adapt their kit to a specific playstyle.
- The [KINETIC] damage is the most common element, provides no additional effects, and only acts as a form of pure damage. It is also worth nothing that, it is the only damage type that can be switched into a different element upon choosing Overclocks and, unless playing as the Weapons Specialist from the Gunner, it is often recommended to change it into a different one whenever the option is present, unless the Survivor already invested into synergistic upgrades to [KINETIC] damage.
- [FIRE] damage is one of three elements capable of dealing status damage to a target, and effectively being the best at it. As such, it is the element of choice for a playstyle based purely on status damage, such as the Interrogator from the Driller. Upgrades related to status damage should be a priority when picking weapons using this element.
- [ELECTRICAL] damage is one of three elements capable of dealing status damage to a target, with this one increasing crit chances to affected target. This element is mostly useful for softening up bugs with direct damage, thanks to the increase in crit chance it provides. However, critical damage and status damage are mutually exclusive, and thus, status damage cannot deal crits. On top of that, crit chance from [ELECTRICAL] damage is capped at 100%, limiting its effectiveness as a supportive element. [ELECTRICAL] should be used in builds that specializes either on crit damage, either on a hybrid of both status damage and crit. Scout benefits the most from this element, thanks to his high crit damage.
- [ACID] damage is one of three elements capable of dealing status damage to a target, with this one increasing all damage taken by the target. Much like [ELECTRICAL] damage, [ACID] damage acts as a softener for dealing more damage, the difference being that it is universally effective for both direct, and status damage. This makes it one of the most reliable damage types in the game, excelling both in status damage, and as a supportive element. However, it is fairly uncommon, as it is the element with the least weapons using it, with only 4 using [ACID] by default, and 1 as an Overclock option. For a build centered around direct damage, [ELECTRICAL] damage pairs well with it, as the damage inflicted to bugs can quickly ramp up when both are combined.
- [COLD] damage acts primarily as a supportive element, impairing the mobility of all bugs affected by it. Bugs that are affected by the [COLD] element, will move slower and, should the effect be more potent, freeze in place, preventing them from moving entirely, rendering them unable to deal damage whatsoever. The Survivor can take advantage of this by running past them unharmed to move away from danger, and in situations of being cornered, can make the difference between life and death. In alternative gamemodes such as Hardcore, this can be extremely valuable.
- [PLASMA] is a unique element which gives its weapons, the ability for its projectiles/beams to bounce off obstacles. This makes this element less reliant on precision for the weapon to hit its targets, and especially useful in confined places. On top of that, weapons who use [PLASMA] by default, can obtain Overclocks which, instead of replacing the [PLASMA] element by another one, add elements of [FIRE], [ELECTRICAL], or [COLD] to it, allowing a single [PLASMA] weapon to use up to 3 different damage types at once, making status damage builds viable with them.

| “ | Faily straight forwards assault rifle that shoots in burstsTargets closest enemyDeals [KINETIC] damage
| — Weapon description
The Deepcore GK2 is one of the weapons available to Scout by default in DRG Survivor. It is the starting weapon for Scout's [Class Mod Classic] Classic class mod.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Deepcore GK2 shoots a volley of 3 bullets that automatically targets the closest enemy.
- The Big Game Hunter balanced overclock, while adding Critical Damage and Critical Chance to the weapon, also modifies the targeting of the weapon to instead target the enemy with the highest HP, within it's targeting range.
## Guide and Strategy
While it can pick off minor Glyphid Grunts at the start of a stage, the Deepcore GK2 is fundamentally a single-target DPS weapon. It excels at focus-firing high-threat targets, Elites, and Bosses. 
As such, the best strategy to succeed with this weapon is to build it for high Fire Rate, Reload Speed, and Damage stats, whilst keeping an eye out for those sweet Piercing upgrades. 
- Generally, the highest-quality upgrade is the best choice, but the priority for cards of equal rarity is Piercing>Damage=Fire Rate>Reload Speed. Keep in mind that Piercing upgrades can never be legendary, so you may need to choose between an epic Piercing and legendary Reload Speed upgrade.
- Equip Gear pieces that can boost your Fire Rate, Reload Speed, and Damage stats.
- Keep an eye out especially for Piercing level-up upgrades, as this heavily mitigates its weakness against swarms, allowing bullets to tear through front-line bugs to hit enemies behind them. Piercing always takes priority unless a higher-quality card is offered.
- Equip overclocks like Big Game Hunter and A Little More Oomph! to increase your DPS and deal devastating damage to high value targets.
- The Deepcore GK2 can also be shifted into elemental archetypes with its overclocks:
- The Battery Bullets overclock swaps the weapon's damage type to [ELECTRICAL], useful for reaching incredible amounts of Critical Chance, and with it, deal Critical Damage much faster.
- The Refrigerated Gunpowder overclock swaps the weapon's damage type to [COLD]. Skipping the Big Game Hunter overclock in favor of this one transforms the weapon into a reliable defensive tool that automatically freezes targets that come close to the player.
- The Favourite unstable overclock will increase the Damage and Fire Rate of your weapon at the cost of Damage and Fire Rate of all other weapons. It is a great choice if you're looking to maintain your Deepcore GK2 as your anchor weapon. Otherwise, Lead Wrapped Ammo can still shred health bars, as long as you keep your Fire Rate and Reload Speed in check.
- Be wary, the Fire Rate penalties of High Caliber Rounds and Lead Wrapped Ammo combine additively, leading to a -50% Fire Rate penalty!
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Deepcore GK2 Weapon Mastery in Crystalline Caverns on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

# Demolitionist  (alias/duplicados: Engineer, Maintenance Worker, Tinkerer)
DRGDRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEngineer
| “ | The smartest of the lot, the Engineer survives by using his gadgets: Turrets, drones, and a lot of plasma make quick work of any hostiles
| — Class description
The Engineer is one of five playable characters in DRG Survivor, unlocking when reaching Player Rank 5.
The Engineer Class
Engineer focuses around area control and lockdown gameplay. This makes him ideal for holding a single area, but struggles during exploration and resourcing. His starting weapons consist of the LMG Gun Platform, "Warthog" Auto 210, "Stubby" Voltaic SMG, and Hi-Volt Thunderbird. His class mods are Maintenance Worker, Tinkerer, and Demolitionist, with Maintenance Worker being unlocked by default.
## Stats
Baseline stats for the Engineer Class are below. Stats may be altered by upgrades or by choosing a Class Mod.

| +XP Gain | 5%
| Critical Chance | 5%
| Critical Damage | 150%
## Available Weapons
Engineer has a set of eleven weapons that, once unlocked by ranking up in the class, can always be found by him during regular dives.
The Maintenance Worker Engineer also has access to all unlocked [CONSTRUCT] weapons, and the Demolitionist has access to all unlocked [EXPLOSIVE] weapons.

The Drak-25 Plasma Carbine is one of the weapons available to the Scout in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 21 on the Scout.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist class mod.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Drak-25 Plasma Carbine Weapon Mastery in Salt Pits on Hazard 4+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

| — Weapon description
The Dragonstorm Incinerator is one of the weapons available to Demolisher in DRG Survivor. It is the default weapon of the [Class Mod Contractor] Contractor class mod.
It can also be equipped while using Driller's [Class Mod Interrogator] Interrogator class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Dragonstorm Incinerator shoots a backward facing beam, leaving behind a [GROUNDZONE]. The beam and groundzone both deal [FIRE] damage and apply Burn stacks.
- The Afterburner balanced overclock can be taken twice.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Dragonstorm Incinerator Weapon Mastery in Crystalline Caverns on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

# ELECTRICAL damage  (alias/duplicados: ELECTRIC damage)
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsDamageElectrical
Other types of damage include [KINETIC], [FIRE], [COLD], [ACID] and [PLASMA]
[ELECTRICAL] is a damage type in DRG Survivor.
[ELECTRICAL] damage applies Shock to the target, with the number of stacks applied equal to an elemental weapon's Potency. Shock increases Crit Chance against targets by 0.125% per stack, up to a max of 100%. It deals moderate damage to a random nearby enemy, 0.5 dmg/stack/tick, and applies 8 stacks to the damaged enemy.

# Egg Hunt
[Egg Hunt Thumbnail]
🚧 PARDON OUR DUST -- This page is under construction 🚧
| “ | Search the caverns for alien eggs, return them to cargo pods, and fight off any who dare interupt your work!
| 
Egg Hunt is a primary mission type Introduced in the Heavy Duty DLC. In this game mode the player must mine eggs and deposit them in the cargo pod. On the final stage depositing the last egg will summon a Brood Nexus, which must be defeated to complete the dive.
## Mission
Egg Hunt missions consist of three stages.
Between stages, the player can access the shop, where they can buy Upgrades.
Partway through each stage, Mission Control points the player towards a Landing Zone (LZ), where they can call down a Supply Pod carrying a selection of game-changing Artifacts.
On the third and final stage, the player must collect four eggs, before taking down a Brood Nexus. 
## Sector Progression
| “ | Behind this gate lies the next sector, and riches untold. However, scans detect a massive outbreak of glyphid activity – far beyond the usual norms. If you want to punch through this horde you'll need to use every trick in the manual.
| 
Egg Hunt is unlocked by owning the Heavy Duty DLC and reaching player rank 15. Each Biome for each Sector becomes available as the player completes Mission Goals ([Biome Point Completed]) in the Egg Hunt mission type. Once enough Mission Goals are completed, the player must clear a special Gate mission to progress to the next Sector.
Higher level sectors have Mutators and minimum Hazard levels. (Sector 02 is Haz 2+, Sector 03 is Haz 3+, etc.)

# Elimination
[Elimination Thumbnail]
🚧 PARDON OUR DUST -- This page is under construction 🚧
| “ | Dive deep and fight your way through a horde of elite enemies in order to draw out and slay the ultimate enemy.
| 
Elimination is a primary mission type in which the player advances through five stages, defeating elite enemies and ultimately facing a Dreadnought.
## Mission
Elimination missions consist of five stages. In each stage, the player fights off a constant trickle of enemies until a larger wave appears. Combat continues until Elite enemies spawn. Once they are defeated, the Drop Pod returns to transport the player deeper. Between stages, the player can access the shop, where they can buy Upgrades.
The amount of waves varies between stages:
[Swarm] Stage 1: One wave
[Swarm] Stage 2: Two waves
[Swarm] Stage 3: Three waves
[Swarm] Stage 4: Three waves
Partway through each stage, Mission Control points the player towards a Landing Zone (LZ), where they can call down a Supply Pod carrying a selection of game-changing Artifacts.
On the fifth and final stage, the player must defeat four Elites, followed by a Dreadnought. If the player only fends off the background trickle of enemies, the Elites and the Dreadnought will eventually emerge from their cocoons on their own. However, players can accelerate this process by locating and manually tearing open the cocoons.
The Dreadnought has a 50% chance of being either a Glyphid Dreadnought, or the Dreadnought Twins (Dreadnought Arbalest and Dreadnought Lacerator).
## Sector Progression
| “ | Behind this gate lies the next sector, and riches untold. However, scans detect a massive outbreak of glyphid activity – far beyond the usual norms. If you want to punch through this horde you'll need to use every trick in the manual.
| 
The Elimination mission type is unlocked by default. Each Biome for each Sector becomes available as the player completes Mission Goals ([Biome Point Completed]) in the Elimination mission type. Once enough Mission Goals are completed, the player must clear a special Gate mission to progress to the next Sector.
Higher level sectors have Mutators and minimum Hazard levels. (Sector 02 is Haz 2+, Sector 03 is Haz 3+, etc.)

# Escort Duty
[Escort Duty Thumbnail]
🚧 PARDON OUR DUST -- This page is under construction 🚧
| “ | Escort a friendly drilldozer into the depths, keeping it fueled and moving until you can find and crack the mysterious Ommoran Heartstone.
| 
Escort Duty is a main mission type where the player escorts a Drilldozer through three stages, finishing with an Ommoran Heartstone boss fight.
## Mission
The player escorts a Drilldozer (B0b-33) across a long, straight stage. At the start of every stage, the dozer's fuel tank will be empty. The player must mine and deliver Oil Shale ([Ressource Oil Shale]) to the dozer, requiring to stand near the visible radius around the dozer to allow it to move forward and progress through the stage. The dozer deals massive damage to enemies in front of it while it's moving.
The first two of the three stages end with arming detonators to clear the way so the Drilldozer can go deeper.
The final stage ends in the Ommoran Heartstone boss fight, where the player must stand in the Drilldozer's radius of influence while surviving waves of enemies. Beamers periodically show up to disable the dozer and must be destroyed to continue. The moment the Ommoran Heartstone is cracked open, all nearby enemies are killed, and the escape Drop Pod is called down, finishing the mission.

## Game Tips
- There's no rush when doing an Escort Duty mission
- Mining the Ommoran Heartstone is hard work - Karl praise heavy machinery!
- The drilldozer is great at drilling through all kinds of rock and stone – just not downwards. That's what C4 is for!

| “ | Shoots large ball of plasma, that bounces on terrainTargets groups of enemiesDeals [PLASMA] damage
| — Weapon description
The Experimental Plasma Charger (EPC) is one of the weapons available to the Driller in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 27 on the Driller.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist and Scout's [Class Mod Recon] Recon class mods.
## Notable Quirks
The EPC has the projectile tag, so Fire Rate upgrades nominally apply to it. It does, however, have a clip size of 1, making such upgrades ineffective.
The "Drippin Balls" Unstable Overclock adds the [FIRE] and [GROUNDZONE] tags to the weapon. Wherever the plasma ball flies, it leaves fiery groundzones beneath it. 
Drippin Balls Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 22 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.1
| Lifetime | 2s | 
| Radius | 1 unit | 
| Potency | 16 Burn | Base Potency / 3
Additionally, while the trails on the ground from the "Drippin Balls" Unstable Overclock do apply status effects, the "Hot Plasma" or "Cold Plasma" Balanced Overclocks must have been taken in order for Experimental Plasma Charger to receive potency upgrades. Without either of these Overclocks, there is little value in trying to build it for status damage.
However, Drippin Balls is extremely strong because the damage dealt by the trails is based off of the damage from the shots themselves.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Experimental Plasma Charger Weapon Mastery in Salt Pits on Hazard 4+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage
## Overclocks

## Availablility
## Always available for
 Gunner

 Gunner Rank 15
## Base stats
## [Damage] Base damage
 80
## [Reload Speed] Reload time
 4s
## [Range] Range
 5 units
## [Knockback] Has knockback
 No
## Constructs
## [Drone] Drones
 2
## Elemental Damage
## [Potency]Potency
 24 Burn
## [Duration] Pool duration
 2s
| “ | Attack drones that seek and destroy nearby enemiesDeals [FIRE] damage
| — Weapon description
The Firefly Hunter Drone is one of the weapons available to the Gunner in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 15 on the Gunner.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker and Demolisher's [Class Mod Operator] Operator class mods.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The player begins with 2 drones, one that circles clockwise and one that circles counterclockwise around the player. The drones circle at a distance of 5 units at 90° degrees a second, applying 80 [FIRE] damage and applying 24 stacks of Burn to whatever enemy they touch in their path. Each drone effectively completes its orbit around the player every 4 seconds. Shall the player acquire any more drones, they shall all circle counterclockwise around the player. 
The Behaviour Chip: Defensive overclock adds two drones; all drones will perfectly circle counterclockwise around the player at all times, much like the default behavior of the Cryo Guard and the Thunderbird drones.
The Crisis Protocol overclock changes the drones' behavior, sending them into a frantic frenzy upon the player taking damage. For 2.5 seconds, the drones will rush to and swarm around their owner in a criss cross formation at 1.5x their normal movement speed, after which they will resume either Defensive or normal behavior, depending if the Defensive OC is present.
The Fuel Leak overclock gives the drones a small trail of fire that leaves Groundzone areas.. It also adds the [GROUNDZONE] tag to the weapon.
Fuel Leak Groundzone Data
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 8 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage / 10
| Lifetime | 2s | 
| Radius | 0.5 units | 
| Potency | 8 Burn | Base Potency / 3
The Disposable Tech Overclock adds the [EXPLOSIVE] and [GROUNDZONE] tags, causes the drones to explode and knock back small enemies at the end of their lifetime, leaving a fiery ground zone with the following stats:
Disposable Tech Explosion Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Base Damage | 200 | Base Damage * 2.5
| Radius | 2.75 units | 
Disposable Tech Explosion Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 8 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage / 10
| Lifetime | 2s | 
| Radius | 2.75 units | 
| Potency | 8 Burn | Base Potency / 3
Disposable Tech also reverses the effect of lifetime upgrades, making them shorten the lifetime of the drone instead of lengthening it and resulting in more explosions and more ground zones. Additionally, lifetime upgrades will increase the duration of the ground zones, making lifetime a very strong stat if you are running Disposable Tech on your drones.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Firefly Hunter Drone Weapon Mastery in Hollow Bough on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Potency
## Overclocks

# Heavy Duty
[Heavy Duty Key Art]
🚧 PARDON OUR DUST -- This page is under construction 🚧
| “ | Your current equipment is insufficient for this Heavy Duty
| 
The Heavy Duty DLC was released on Steam on 30th of April 2026 and later on Android and IOS on 16th of June 2026. It contains one new class the Demolisher, with its 3 class mods and 11 weapons, the Glacial Strata biome and the Egg Hunt gamemode.
## Steam Description
The mines are about to get a whole lot louder.
Introducing the Heavy Duty expansion for Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor. 
NEW CLASS: DEMOLISHER
Built for destruction. Engineered for overkill.
This new powerhouse class operates a fully weaponized industrial vehicle known as the Rockdozer, turning the battlefield into a demolition site. Crush the swarms beneath your wheels, leaving a trail of devastation. If the other dwarves fight the bugs…the Demolisher plows them down.
The Demolisher brings an arsenal of 11 new weapons with him to Hoxxes, available to other classes via the tag system
NEW BIOME: GLACIAL STRATA
Glacial Strata introduces a fast-paced frozen biome where the environment is as powerful as your arsenal. Slide across smooth ice for bursts of speed at the cost of control, trigger deadly icicle traps by shattering ice crystals, and face new cryo-infused enemies adapted to the cold. 
With more biome choices added to missions, and without increasing progression requirements, you gain greater freedom to tackle challenges your way.
NEW MISSION: EGG HUNT
Collect alien eggs and have Bosco deliver them to a central cargo pod in a star-shaped flow, forcing you to branch out and return under fire. Once you hit the quota, a short grace period lets you risk it all for extra rewards… if you dare.
Deliver the final egg, and everything changes. A Brood Nexus erupts on top of the pod, locking down your exit and triggering a multi-phase boss fight. It spawns relentless waves of enemies, and at key moments, creates Nexus Sprouts that must be destroyed before you can damage the core again.

| “ | Defensive drones that damage nearby enemiesDeals [ELECTRICAL] damage
| — Weapon description
The Hi-Volt Thunderbird is a weapon unlocked along with the Engineer class when reaching Player Rank 5.
It can also be equipped while using Demolisher's [Class Mod Operator] Operator class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The player begins with 3 drones that circle counterclockwise around the player at a distance of 5 units at 60° degrees a second. The drones can shoot up to 3 beams, one for each nearby enemy, each doing 26 [ELECTRIC] damage and applying 14 stacks of Shock. Each drone effectively completes its orbit around the player every 6 seconds.
The Behaviour Chip: Aggro Overclock changes the drones' behavior. They will rotate around the player as normal until an enemy appears within range, after which they will attack the enemy or groups of enemies in a circular motion. Should the player wander too far from the drones, they will try to return to the player and resume circling.
The Conduit overclock works as such: constructs with this overclock tick every 0.5 seconds and attempt to get the closest friendly construct within a 5 range radius. If it finds a friendly construct, it fires a beam between the targets that deals half of the weapon's damage, but applies the full potency amount. Friendly targets are able to shoot each other, so if you have two drones next to each other, it would result in 2 beams being fired.
The Disposable Tech Overclock adds the [EXPLOSIVE] and [GROUNDZONE] tags, causes the drones to explode and knock back small enemies at the end of their lifetime, leaving electrified ground zone with the following stats:
Disposable Tech Explosion Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Base Damage | 117 | Base Damage * 4.5
| Radius | 4 units | 
Disposable Tech Explosion Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 3 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage / 10, rounded up
| Lifetime | 3.5s | 
| Radius | 4 units | 
| Potency | 5 Shock | Base Potency / 3, rounded up
Disposable Tech also reverses the effect of lifetime upgrades, making them shorten the lifetime of the drone instead of lengthening it and resulting in more explosions and more ground zones. Additionally, lifetime upgrades will increase the duration of the ground zones, making lifetime a very strong stat if you are running Disposable Tech on your drones.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Hi-Volt Thunderbird Weapon Mastery in Hollow Bough on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Potency

| [OC Balanced][Potency Up] | Potent Juice | +30% Potency | Greatly increase potency

The Impact Axe is one of the weapons available to the Driller in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 12 on the Driller.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Demolitionist] Demolitionist class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
- The Impact Axes travel a certain distance, deal explosive [KINETIC] damage equivalent to 1.5x base damage when they land, and then return back to the player after their "clip" is emptied, knocking aside enemies along the way. Until they return, their landing points are marked on the ground with an indicator.
- If the player takes the "Corrosive Coating" or the "Diesel Soaked" overclock, the Impact Axes will leave a trail (of [ACID] or [FIRE], respectively) both during its landing and its return. They do not change the explosive damage to [ACID] or [FIRE].
Corrosive Coating Trail Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 27 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.3
| Lifetime | 2s | 
| Radius | 0.75 units | 
| Potency | 10 Corrode | Base Potency * 0.5'
Diesel Soaked Trail Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 27 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.3
| Lifetime | 2s | 
| Radius | 0.75 units | 
| Potency | 10 Burn | Base Potency * 0.5
- While the trails on the ground from Corrosive Coating and Diesel Soaked overclocks do apply status effect stacks, the Impact Axe can't receive Potency upgrades, meaning that there is little value in trying to build it for status damage. However, these two overclocks are extremely strong because the damage they deal is based off of the damage from the axes themselves.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Impact Axe Weapon Mastery in Magma Core on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Weapon Range

The Thick Boy Overclock turns the weapon's clip of 2 (or 4 with Bigger Mags) into a clip of 1, making fire rate become dependent on Reload Speed rather than the Fire Rate stat.
The math behind Thick Boy:
Damage: Clip size * damage
Reload speed: -0.6 + (1/clip size)

The K1-P Viper Drone is one of the weapons available to the Driller and Engineer in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 24 on the Driller.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker and Demolisher's [Class Mod Operator] Operator class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The player begins with 2 drones, one that circles clockwise and one that circles counterclockwise around the player. Each drone circles at a distance of 4 units at 120° degrees a second, applying 60 [ACID] damage and applying 24 stacks of Corrode to whatever enemy they touch in their path. Each drone effectively completes its orbit around the player every 3 seconds and leaves a pool wherever it flies. Shall the player acquire any more drones, they shall all circle clockwise around the player. 
Acid Drone Pool Data
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Lifetime | 2s | 
| Damage | 6 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.1
| Radius | 0.5 units | 
| Potency | 8 Corrode | Base Potency / 3
The Behaviour Chip: Defensive overclock adds two drones; all drones will perfectly circle clockwise around the player at all times, much like the default behavior of the Cryo Guard and the Thunderbird drones.
The Crisis Protocol Overclock changes the drones' behavior, sending them into a frantic frenzy upon the player taking damage. For 2.5 seconds, the drones will rush to and swarm around their owner in a criss cross formation at 1.5x their normal movement speed, after which they will resume either Defensive or normal behavior, depending if the Defensive OC is present.
The Disposable Tech Overclock adds the [EXPLOSIVE] tag, causes the drones to explode at the end of their lifetime, and leave an acidic, bubbling ground zone that deals damage over time. The explosion and groundzone stats are as follows:
Disposable Tech Explosion Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Base Damage | 180 | Base Damage * 3
| Radius | 2.75 units | 
Disposable Tech Explosion Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 6 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.1
| Lifetime | 2.5s | 
| Radius | 2.75 units | 
| Potency | 8 Corrode | Base Potency / 3
Disposable Tech also reverses the effect of lifetime upgrades, making them shorten the lifetime of the drone instead of lengthening it and resulting in more explosions and more ground zones. Additionally, lifetime upgrades will increase the duration of the ground zones, making lifetime a very strong stat if you are running Disposable Tech on your drones.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the K1-P Viper Drone Weapon Mastery in Hollow Bough on Hazard 4+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Potency

The Kaisong Scissor Ray is one of the weapons available to Demolisher in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching class rank 24 on the Demolisher.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Kaisong Scissor Ray releases two beams that move back and forth in an 'X' formation, similar to the cutting motion of scissors.
- The Widget Spinner overclock now allows the beams to have a 360 degree rotation, instead of the back and forth motion.
- The Diffractor Prism allows the beams to bounce off the terrain, similar to the Shard Diffractor.
## Known bugs
- The Widget Spinner and Diffractor Prism can be offered twice, even when already taken.
- The weapon's stats screen displays some [ACID] Groundzone effect info, despite the weapon not having the [GROUNDZONE] tag, or dealing Acid damage.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Kaisong Scissor Ray Weapon Mastery in Magma Core on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage
## Overclocks

# Knockout
[Knockout Key Art]
🚧 No Peeking -- This page is about content that is still in development and is subject to change 🚧
🚧 PARDON OUR DUST -- This page is under construction 🚧
| “ | Management has ordered a more Hands-on approach!
| 
The Knockout expansion currently has an unknown release date. It contains a new class the Brawler, with its 3 class mods and 11 weapons, the Sandblasted Corridors biome and the Brood Harvest gamemode.
## Steam Description
Management has ordered a more Hands-on approach!
Introducing the Knockout expansion for Deep Rock Galactic: Survivor.
NEW CLASS: BRAWLER
No need for pickaxes when you have fists of steel!
Knockout the swarm with up close and personal weaponry!
The Brawler brings 11 new weapons to Hoxxes ready to smash, tear and crush his way deep into the planet
As a special bonus DRG has decreed that this new arsenal will also be available to to the other classes already fighting the good fight on Hoxxes, via the tag system, in order to help their mining efforts
NEW BIOME: SANDBLASTED CORRIDORS
Dry and inhospitable, but rich in resources
The only vegetation is out to get you and even the environment shifts as twisters scour the landscape, as if to keep you out. But brave dwarves who can find their way and gain the upper hand can gain riches beyond measure
Once again more biome choices added to missions, and without increasing progression requirements, you gain even greater freedom to tackle challenges your way.
NEW MISSION: BROOD HARVEST
Something stirs in the deepest caverns of Hoxxes and Management have acquired samples from new glyphid lifeforms that show great potential, but the R&D department requires more to further the research!
Collect as many samples of Kytoplasm from the Broodlings as you can, dive deep and find the source if you can and gather as much as you can before you are overwhelmed
The more Kytoplasm you can extract the more support you’ll get during your dive, but be careful, the indigenous glyphids are very protective of the broodlings
The few who make it to the last stage, will face off against the Dreadnought Empress and her brood, in a full stage fight for survival!

The Krakatoa Sentinel is one of the weapons available to Driller by default in DRG Survivor.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Krakatoa Sentinel is a Turret that fires a beam that deals [FIRE] damage and applies Burn to enemies hit. This weapon can gain more beams through an overclock or with a 'Split Nozzles' legendary beam upgrade.
- Turret amount: There seems to be no cap on the number of turrets that can be active at once, only limited by your [CONSTRUCT] lifetime and reload speed stats. Upgrading these two stats can lead to a pop-up army capable of holding any line.
- Tank Tracks: This overclock allows [CONSTRUCT] weapons to follow the player instead of remaining stationary. They will move at 43% of the player's Move Speed.
- Mining Directive: This overclock allows the [CONSTRUCT] to mine terrain and minerals. They will shift their focus towards terrain and minerals if no enemies are within targeting range.
- Krakatoa Protocol: This overclock gives the Krakatoa the [EXPLOSIVE], [GROUNDZONE], and [ELECTRICAL] tags. The Krakatoa explodes, dealing 10 damage and knockback, and it leaves two ground zones in its place, one fire, and one electric. Both ground zones have the same stats.
Krakatoa Protocol Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 2 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.125, rounded up
| Lifetime | 5s | 
| Radius | 3.5 units | 
| Potency | 5 Burn and 5 Shock | Base Potency / 2
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Krakatoa Sentinel Weapon Mastery in Azure Weald on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Weapon Range
## Overclocks

The LMG Gun Platform is one of the weapons available to Engineer by default in DRG Survivor. It is the starting weapon of the [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker class mod. 
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The LMG Gun Platform is a Turret that shoots [KINETIC] bullet projectiles to enemies within targeting range. The weapon's damage type can be modified with overclocks.
- The LMG Gun Platform doesn’t have a clip size — it will continue shooting at enemies until its lifetime runs out. This makes Fire Rate upgrades valuable, as they increase the number of projectiles fired before the turrets disappear.
- Turret amount: There seems to be no cap on the number of turrets that can be active at once, only limited by your [CONSTRUCT] lifetime and reload speed stats. Upgrading these two stats can lead to a pop-up army capable of holding any line.
- Tank Tracks: This overclock allows [CONSTRUCT] weapons to follow the player instead of remaining stationary. They will move at 43% of the player's Move Speed.
- Mining Directive: This overclock allows the [CONSTRUCT] to mine terrain and minerals. They will shift their focus towards terrain and minerals if no enemies are within targeting range.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the LMG Gun Platform Weapon Mastery in Crystalline Caverns on Hazard 2+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Reload Speed

# Lasting Weapon
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsFiring TypesLasting
[LASTING] is a firing type in DRG Survivor. For a weapon to be considered [LASTING], it must have a lifetime, meaning the weapon in question primarily does not fire ammo with a certain number of clips or mags until it needs to reload. Instead, the weapon activates and continues to fire until its lifetime runs out, after which it will then reload. A perfect example of lasting weapons are those within the [DRONE] weapon family; Drones automatically circle around the player or attack enemies until they exhaust their lifetime.
Other firing types include: [PRECISE], [SPRAY], [AREA], [BEAM]

# Lethal Operations
| [Drg stub] | This article is a stub. You can help Deep Rock Galactic Wiki by expanding it.
This article is a stub. You can help Deep Rock Galactic Wiki by expanding it.
Lethal Operations are a special type of dive that players can undertake in the game. Lethal Operations are weekly missions that puts the player on an Elimination mission with 1 beneficial Mutator and 3 negative Mutators. They can be unlocked after the player completes a dive on Hazard 4, and can be played up to Hazard 5.
## Restrictions
Vanguard Contracts can contain certain restrictions. Some restrictions include:
- Biome
- For example, Magma Core.
- Starter weapon
- For example, start with the Firefly Hunter Drone.
- Equippable weapons
- For example, can only equip [CONSTRUCT] weapons.
- Unequippable weapons
- For example, cannot equip [PROJECTILE] weapons.
## Completion
Lethal Operations reset once every seven days, at the same time of day as Vanguard Contracts. Completing a Lethal Operation grants the player [XP Gain] +200% Rank XP and [Upgrades icon] Credits as a First Win Bonus. This bonus only applies for the first time the player completes the Lethal Operation for the week.
Additionally, the player is guaranteed to receive an Epic piece of Gear when defeating the boss.

The M1000 Classic is one of the weapons available to Scout in DRG Survivor. Unlocked by reaching level 3 on the Scout.
Shoots high caliber, heavily piercing bullets. It's ideal to kill Elite enemies hiding in a big swarm.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist class mod.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the M1000 Classic Weapon Mastery in Crystalline Caverns on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage
## Overclocks
| Icon | Name | Effects | Game description
| [OC Balanced][Reload Damage] | A Little More Oomph! | +25% Damage+25% Reload Speed | Increases damage and reload speed
| [OC Balanced][Bigger Mag] | Bigger Mags | +100% Clip Size-10% Reload Speed | Increases the clip size at the cost of reload speed
| [OC Balanced][Reload Firerate] | Gas Rerouting | +30% Fire Rate+30% Reload Speed | Increase fire rate and reload speed
| [OC Balanced][Damage Fire] | Pan Fried Shells | Change the damage type to [FIRE] | —
| [OC Balanced][Knockback] | Rubber Tip | +15% Damage+Knockback | Knock back enemies
| [OC Unstable][Sawn Off] | Sawn-Off | -50% Weapon Range-10 Accuracy | More bullets, less range
| [OC Unstable][Fatboy] | Thick Boy | | Combines all your projectiles into one thick boy, greatly increasing damage while reducing reload speed
The Thick Boy Overclock turns the weapon's clip of 6 (or 12 with Bigger Mags) into a clip of 1, making fire rate become dependent on Reload Speed rather than the Fire Rate stat.
The math behind Thick Boy:
Damage: Clip size * damage
Reload speed: -0.6 + (1/clip size)

# Mini Exploder
These creatures will run directly at the character. They have relatively low health. On death or when they touch you, they do a tiny explosion. If you turn towards them just as they enter your melee range, you will knock them back and they will explode at a safe distance. If you have many chasing you, knocking two of them into the swarm with melee, is enough damage to pop others.

The Nishanka Boltshark X-80 is one of the weapons available to the Scout in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching Rank 24 on the Scout.
It can also be equipped while using Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist, Driller's [Class Mod Interrogator] Interrogator, and Demolisher's [Class Mod Gridrunner] Gridrunner class mods.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Nishanka Boltshark X-80 Weapon Mastery in Azure Weald on Hazard 4+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Potency
## Overclocks
| Icon | Name | Effects | Game description
| [OC Balanced][Damage Cryo] | Cryo Bolt | — | Adds a Cryo bolt to the rotation
| [OC Balanced][Damage Fire] | Fire Bolt | — | Adds a Fire bolt to the rotation
| [OC Balanced][Reload Firerate] | Gas Rerouting | +30% Fire Rate+30% Reload Speed | Increase fire rate and reload speed
| [OC Balanced][Knockback] | Rubber Tip | +15% Damage+Knockback | Knock back enemies
| [OC Unstable][Volley] | Bolt Volley | — | Shoot all bolt types at once!
| [OC Unstable][Payload] | Payload | — | When hitting large enemies or rocks, arrows leave behind an electrical field that deals damage
| [OC Unstable][Storme Emag] | Storm E-Mag | +10% Reload Speed+10% Fire Rate | The Last bullet fires in all direction[sic]
## Notable Quirks
By default, every shot from the Boltshark applies both Shock and Corrode statuses. If an OC is taken that adds a bolt to the rotation, then every shot from the Boltshark will apply that new status effect as well - for example, if both Fire Bolt and Cryo Bolt OCs are taken, then every shot will apply Shock, Corrode, Burn, and Slow statuses at the same time. This makes potency and status effect damage very strong upgrades for the Boltshark.
The "Bolt Volley" Unstable Overclock changes the Boltshark to burst-fire mode, like the GK2 or BRT7 - every "shot" becomes a burst of rounds equal in size to the number of bolts in the rotation (so anywhere from 2 to 4 shots per burst). All bolts in the burst are fired in the same direction, like the "Thunderhead" Heavy Autocannon.
If there are 4 bolts in the rotation, then the Boltshark will still fire two bursts of 4 rounds each before reloading - effectively increasing the clip size to 8. Additionally, the weapon will NOT wait for the previous burst to complete before firing the next one, so with enough fire rate you can have multiple bursts overlapping.
Bolt Volley lists -30% Firerate as an additional effect, but effective firerate will still be far higher than without it.
The "Payload" Unstable Overclock will cause each shot to explode if it hits a large target or terrain - this explosion deals damage in an area equal to the damage from the shot itself, applies electric status to all targets hit, and also deals a small amount of mining damage to any terrain in range.
## Voice Lines
| | It's the pointiest pick!

# Nitra  (alias/duplicados: Resources)
 SurvivorResources
Resources are mined in dives. They reward experience and Credits when obtained.
## Meta Resources
Six minerals are used to purchase semi-permanent upgrades from the main menu. They can be obtained by mining their ore nodes during a dive, where they occur in groups of one. They can be found in greater abundance in certain biomes.
| Resource | Stats Upgraded | Rich Biomes
| [Ressource Bismor] Bismor | Max HP | Damage | Reload Speed | Crystalline Caverns, Salt Pits, Glacial Strata
| [Ressource Croppa] Croppa | Armor | Mining Speed | Fire Rate | Magma Core, Azure Weald
| [Ressource Enor Pearl] Enor Pearl | Pickup Radius | XP Gain | Potency | Hollow Bough, Salt Pits (none in Magma Core)
| [Ressource Jadiz] Jadiz | Starting Nitra | Critical Chance | Critical Damage | Crystalline Caverns, Glacial Strata
| [Ressource Magnite] Magnite | Starting Gold | Status Effect Damage | Life Regen | Magma Core
| [Ressource Umanite] Umanite | Luck | Movement Speed | Artifact Reroll & Price | Hollow Bough, Azure Weald
Minerals can be bought and sold for Credits at the Mineral Market, on the Upgrades screen of the main menu. Buying costs [Ressource Credits]400 per mineral and selling returns [Ressource Credits]200.
## Consumable Minerals
Gold, Nitra, and Red Sugar are mid-dive resources. Gold and Nitra are converted to XP at the end of a dive.
Red Sugar ([Ressource Red Sugar]) heals the player when it's picked up and can be obtained by mining its glowing red ore nodes. The Red Sugar Cube artifact makes collecting Red Sugar increase the dwarf's Max HP stat by 2 for every chunk collected.
Gold ([Ressource Gold]) can be obtained: 
- By mining its ore veins
- By mining any plain rock with a Gold Scanner active
- By popping Lootbugs and Huuli Hoarders
- By completing secondary objectives
Gold can be used:
- To buy player stat upgrades and tag mastery upgrades between dive stages
- To heal 50% between dive stages
- To re-roll the selection of upgrade cards between dive stages or upon leveling up
- To benefit certain artifacts like Gold-Tipped Bullets and the Pay2Win Console
Nitra ([Ressource Nitra]) can be obtained:
- By mining its ore veins
- By mining any plain rock with a Nitra Scanner active
- By popping Lootbugs and Huuli Hoarders
Nitra can be used:
- To buy weapon upgrades between dive stages
- To re-roll the artifact card selection from the Supply Pod
- To benefit certain artifacts like Nitragenic Powder and Pickled Nitra
Note that on the final stage of a dive, the only uses of Gold and Nitra becomes to re-roll upgrade cards (from leveling up), to re-roll artifact cards (from the Supply Pod), and to benefit certain artifacts.
## Oil Shale
Oil Shale ([Ressource Oil Shale]) is a special resource for the Escort Duty mission type. The player must mine and collect Oil Shale in order to fuel the Drilldozer and continue the mission.
 - 
 [Oil Shale]
 Oil Shale
## Morkite Reactor Cores
Message from the main menu
Morkite Reactor Cores ([Ressource Reactor Core]) were a rare resource that could be found during Masteries and Anomaly Dives in the Early Access versions of the game. They appeared as large triangular boxes with glowing cores.
They have been removed from the game as part of the 1.0 Update.

# Objectives
| [Drg stub] | This article is a stub. You can help Deep Rock Galactic Wiki by expanding it.
This article is a stub. You can help Deep Rock Galactic Wiki by expanding it.
## Primary Objectives
## Elimination
Every stage except the last has an Elite Creature that players must slay to continue to the next stage. The amount of Elites depends on Hazard level and Mission type. The Alien Threat Level timer will begin to tick once the Elites spawn, and will start to increase the Alien Threat Level if the player takes too long to kill the Elites. If the player is fast enough in killing the Elites, they will be rewarded with either a Legendary, Epic or Rare Loot Crate. If the player takes too long, however, they will receive no reward.
Once the Elites have been eliminated, the Drop Pod will arrive for extraction towards the next stage.
The last stage has a Dreadnought that must be drawn out by destroying the Elite Cocoons that appear around the map. Each cocoon will spawn an Elite Creature, which the player can manually pop by damaging it enough, or by killing enough bugs, which will make the Elites pop out of their cocoon automatically. Once all the cocoons have been destroyed, the player can also manually pop the Dreadnought Cocoon, or kill enough bugs for the Dreadnought to come out on its own. The Alien Threat Level timer will begin to tick once the Dreadnought spawns, and will start to increase the Alien Threat Level if the player takes too long to kill the Dreadnought. Once the Dreadnought has been eliminated, it will drop a piece of Gear, and the Drop Pod will arrive for extraction and end the dive (or continue to the next stage, if done on Stage 5 of a Biome Mastery).
 - 
 [Killing the Elite Creature]
 Killing the Elite Creature
## Escort Duty
Escort Duty missions consist of escorting the Drilldozer Unit B0b-33 toward the end of the stage. The player must collect Oil Shale scattered around the map to fuel the Drilldozer and allow it to advance forward. When the Drilldozer reaches certain points of the map, it will trigger either a Swarm, or the Supply Pod. The Alien Threat Level timer will begin to tick as soon as the mission starts, and will start to increase the Alien Threat Level if the player takes too long to finish the stage.
In the first two stages, once the Drilldozer reaches the end of the stage, the player must arm explosives by hitting certain modules around the explosives with their pickaxe, which will be shown in the Minimap. Once all explosives are fully armed, these will trigger an explosion that damages nearby enemies, and commence the Drop Pod extraction sequence. If the player is fast enough in arming the explosives, they will be rewarded with either a Legendary, Epic or Rare Loot Crate which appears near the Drop Pod. If the player takes too long, however, they will receive no reward.
In the last stage, once the Drilldozer reaches the end of the stage, it will be met with the Ommoran Shell, which the Drilldozer will begin to dig into. The player must stay inside the radius near the Drilldozer to allow it to damage the Ommoran Shell. After a certain amount of damage has been done to the Ommoran Shell, it will begin to attack the Drilldozer using Ommoran Beamers. While the beamers are active, the Drilldozer will not be able to damage the Ommoran Shell. As such, the player must go out and mine the beamers with their pickaxe to destroy them. When all beamers are destroyed, the Drilldozer will continue damaging the Ommoran Shell, so long as the player returns to its radius.
Once the Ommoran Shell has been destroyed, it will cause an explosion that instantly kills all active enemies, and drop a piece of Gear and the Ommoran Heartstone, which the player must collect to trigger the Drop Pod extraction sequence.
 - 
 [Escorting the Drilldozer]
 Escorting the Drilldozer
 - 
 [Arming the explosives]
 Arming the explosives
 - 
 [Damaging the Ommoran Shell]
 Damaging the Ommoran Shell
 - 
 [Destroying the Ommoran Beamers]
 Destroying the Ommoran Beamers
## Secondary Objectives
Secondary objectives are tasks that are not required to be completed in order to finish the mission, but doing so rewards the player with Gold and Experience. They are not available initially, but are unlocked once the player collects a cumulative total of 100 Gold during dives.
Secondary objectives do not appear on the final stage of any dive, and do not appear in Class Mod Masteries at all.
| Possible Secondary Objectives:
| [Ressource Apoca Bloom] Collect 6 Apoca Blooms
| [Ressource Boolo Cap] Collect 12 Boolo Caps
| [Ressource Morkite] Collect 20 Morkite

# PLASMA damage  (alias/duplicados: Plasma)
DRG
DRG:RC
DRG:S
 SurvivorDwarvesEquipmentWeaponsDamagePlasma
Other types of damage include [KINETIC], [FIRE], [COLD], [ACID] and [ELECTRICAL]
[PLASMA] is a damage type in DRG Survivor.
[PLASMA] projectiles bounce off terrain and bugs.
## Interactions
## Pierce
Similarly to [KINETIC] projectiles, [PLASMA] projectiles also benefit from Piercing. However, it uses a formula to determine probability to pierce, rather than guaranteeing a certain number of pierces.
- First, the game generates a random number between 0 to 1.
- Next, it takes the current pierce value of the projectile and divides it by 30. If the result is greater than 1, it sets the result to 1.
- This result is then multiplied by 0.7 and adds 0.2.
- This final result is then compared to the random number. If this final result is higher or equal to the random number, the projectile pierces. Otherwise, it ricochets off of the enemy.
This means that a pierce value of 30 or more has a 90% chance to pierce, and a 10% chance to ricochet. As such, some projectiles will pierce into a swarm, exhaust its piercing value, and bounce around inside the swarm dealing damage on each bounce until the projectile's range is exhausted.

# Piercing
[Punch] Piercing is a property of [PROJECTILE] weapons that allow their projectiles to go through bugs, dealing full damage to each bug that it passes through. This makes Piercing a very useful stat against swarms of enemies, and less effective against Elites and Bosses, as it does not directly increase damage against a single target.
## How it works
All enemies have a "pierce value", which ranges between 0 and 4, and it determines how much they resist piercing. This value is subtracted to the projectile that passes through them, and when the projectile's Piercing value reaches 0, it can no longer pierce.
For example, for a weapon with a Piercing stat of 4 passing through basic Grunts (which have a pierce value of 1), its projectiles will go through three Grunts and disappear when it hits the fourth (still dealing full damage to it). In contrast, Praetorian Elites have a pierce value of 4, so the projectile would hit the Praetorian Elite and not pierce it at all. However, if the player obtains a "+25% Piercing" upgrade (increasing the piercing stat from 4 to 5), then the projectile would go through the Praetorian Elite and stop when it hits a Grunt behind it.
It is important to understand that different weapons have different base Piercing values, and thus have different inherent effectiveness against hordes of bugs. If a weapon has a Piercing value of e.g. 20 or more, it will already almost always go through all bugs in almost any swarm, and further increasing its Piercing is unlikely to be advantageous.
It is also important to understand that an e.g. "+100% Piercing" upgrade would simply double the weapon's base Piercing stat; it would not mean that it will now pierce an infinite amount of enemies.

The Proximity Mines is one of the weapons available to Demolisher by default in DRG Survivor.
It can also be equipped while using Driller's [Class Mod Strong Armed] Strong Armed, Engineer's [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker and Engineer's [Class Mod Demolitionist] Demolitionist class mods.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Proximity Mines are explosive mines which the dwarves deploy on the ground. If an enemy touches a mine, it will detonate and deal considerable amounts of explosive damage. These mines have no lifetime stat, meaning they can remain in the ground indefinitely until triggered.
- The base number of mines dropped per attack is 4, but it can be increased with the More Mines balanced overclock.
- The Corrosive Thunder balanced overclock gives the weapon the [GROUNDZONE] tag. However, groundzone effect info is not displayed in the weapon's stats screen. Each mine leaves two groundzones upon exploding: one acid, and one electric, both with the same stats.
Corrosive Thunder Groundzone Stats (Acid and Electric)
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 6 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.1
| Lifetime | 5s | 
| Radius | 3 units | 
| Potency | 10 Corrode and 10 Shock | Base Potency * 0.5
- Despite having the [CONSTRUCT] tag, the Conduit overclock does not shoot electrical beams towards the mines.

| “ | Drops Seismic Repulsors that knock back enemiesStand still to deploy turretsDeals [KINETIC] damage
| — Weapon description
The Seismic Repulsor is one of the weapons available to Gunner in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching class rank 24 on the Gunner.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Seismic Repulsor is a Turret which, when placed, periodically emit a 'thump' attack which deals damage and knocks back enemies.
- Turret amount: There seems to be no cap on the number of turrets that can be active at once, only limited by your [CONSTRUCT] lifetime and reload speed stats. Upgrading these two stats can lead to a pop-up army capable of holding any line.
- Reload Speed: In addition to generating charges faster, reload speed also decreases the time it takes for individual Repulsors to charge their next thump, effectively increasing the fire rate of this weapon.
- Explosion Radius: Explosion radius from gear and upgrades increases each thump's radius.
- Enemies with especially high movement speed or knockback resistance values may walk through a Repulsor before it thumps, causing them to be pushed towards the player. Generally, larger enemies have higher knockback resistance. * The only non-stationary enemies that are fully resistant to knockback are the Elite Praetorian and Q'ronar Mini Elite.
- Coolant Leak: This overclock adds the [GROUNDZONE] tag to the weapon. The groundzone deals [COLD] damage and applies Slow, but the overclock does not add the [COLD] tag to the weapon.
Coolant Leak Groundzone Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 2 every 0.5s/tick | Base Damage * 0.1 (rounded up)
| Lifetime | 12s | 
| Radius | 2 units | 
| Potency | 5 Slow | Base Potency * 0.5
- Disposable Tech: This overclock adds the [EXPLOSION] tag to the weapon. Thumpers will explode at the end of their lifetime, dealing double the base damage in a radius of 4 units. Unlike the Shock Fence, it does not gain the [GROUNDZONE] tag or leave a groundzone behind.
Disposable Tech Explosion Stats
| Stat Name | Stat | Calculation
| Damage | 36 | Base Damage * 2
| Radius | 4 units | 
- Tank Tracks: This overclock allows [CONSTRUCT] weapons to follow the player instead of remaining stationary. They will move at 43% of the player's Move Speed.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Seismic Repulsor Weapon Mastery in Azure Weald on Hazard 4+ awards the following bonuses:
[Mastery Point] +7% Damage, +7% Reload Speed

## Notable Quirks
## Pivot Point and Aiming
The Shard Diffractor does a check to get an "average" direction (area) to "fire", where it overlays a circle based on the weapon range (radius of the circle) and looks at the bugs within the circle. This "average" direction is effectively a weighted approximation of the area where the most bugs are. 
This does not mean it places the pivot point where the highest amount of bugs are, but instead it assigns a weight to every direction and places the pivot point after "averaging" all of the weights.
After determining the direction, the pivot point is placed at a distance of 5 from the player and the beams "fire" in the direction of the pivot point. Usually this pivot point will appear behind your dwarf as the bugs are following behind you, but depending on where the bugs are, your weapon range, movement speed, terrain setup, etc. it can appear to be offset (always at a distance of 5) at other angles.

The Slither Drones is one of the weapons available to Demolisher in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching class rank 6 on the Demolisher. It is the starting weapon for the [Class Mod Operator] Operator class mod.
It can also be equipped while using Engineer's [Class Mod Maintenance Worker] Maintenance Worker class mod.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Slither Drones are a starting group of 6 drones that rotate in a circle formation behind the player, despite the name. These drones can mine any terrain they fly through, and deal [KINETIC] damage.
- Even with all More Drones overclocks (for a total of 14), a full rotation of the drones will not be enough to mine a piece of terrain by itself.
## Mastery Bonuses

The Springloaded Ripper is one of the weapons available to Demolisher in DRG Survivor. It is unlocked by reaching class rank 21 on the Demolisher.
It can also be equipped while using Driller's [Class Mod Strong Armed] Strong Armed, Gunner's [Class Mod Weapons Specialist] Weapons Specialist and Scout's [Class Mod Recon] Recon class mods.
## Weapon Mechanics and Interactions
The Springloaded Ripper shoots out highly damaging blades towards the direction the player is facing.
- The Cold Blades balanced overclock adds the [COLD] tag, the Acid Blades balanced overclock adds the [ACID] tag, and The Reaper unstable overclock adds the [EXPLOSIVE] tag.
- The Reaper does not add each projectile's damage up like the Thick Boy overclock. Instead, it simply shoots one projectile with heavily increased damage that explodes at the end of its lifetime. The explosion radius for it is not displayed in the weapon's stats screen.
- With the Storm E-Mag unstable overclock, the last shot fires 24 total projectiles.
## Mastery Bonuses
Completing the Springloaded Ripper Weapon Mastery in Magma Core on Hazard 3+ awards the following bonus:
[Mastery Point] +12% Damage

# Stats
⚠️HARD HAT REQUIRED – This page is under construction!⚠️
## Calculations
When a stat is modified from its base value, it follows the formula
Stat = ((Base * Skill [icon Modifier Weapons]) + Flat [icon Modifier Flat]) * Meta [icon Modifier Meta] * Overclock [icon Modifier Overclocks] * Artifact [icon Modifier Artifacts]
where Base is a weapon's base stat, Skill [icon Modifier Weapons] is the sum of all modifiers from Upgrade Cards, Flat [icon Modifier Flat] is the sum of all flat (non-percentile) modifiers (like +1 to drone count), Meta [icon Modifier Meta] is the sum of Meta Upgrades + Class modifiers + Weapon Mastery modifiers, and Overclock [icon Modifier Overclocks] and Artifact [icon Modifier Artifacts] are the sum of all Overclock and Artifact modifiers, respectively.
These calculations can be viewed for equipped weapons from the pause screen during a dive.
Typically the game presents stat changes in either yellow text or blue text. These indicate multiplicative stats and additive stats, respectively.
## Stat Explanations
## Weapon Stats
- Beams: Number of beams for [BEAM] weapons
- Charges: For [CONSTRUCT] weapons, how many drones/turrets/etc. the player can keep in their pocket at a time; when the player stands still, charges are deployed in quick succession; while moving, the last charge is auto-deployed if charges would over-fill
- Clip size: How many projectiles a weapon can fire before needing to be reloaded
- Damage: Damage inflicted per projectile[What precisely is this for beam weapons?]
- Explosion radius: Maximum radius covered by [EXPLOSIVE] weapons
- Fire rate: Projectiles fired per second, and how fast a clip is emptied
- Lifetime: How long [LASTING] weapons remain active; for [BEAM] weapons, it's how long it takes to empty a tank
- Piercing: How much "HP" a projectile has; passing through enemies removes HP, and passing through elites and bosses removes extra HP
- Potency: Amount of status effect stacks applied by [FIRE] [ELECTRICAL] [COLD] and [ACID] weapons
- Reload time: Downtime after a clip is emptied before it can be fired again; affects speed of deploying [CONSTRUCT] weapons
- Weapon range: Distance covered by [BEAM] and [PROJECTILE] weapons
## Player Stats
Support Stats
- Max HP: How much damage the player can take before dying
- Life Regen
- Armor: Reduces damage taken, following the formula
dmgReduction = armorValue / (armorValue + 10 *(stageIndex + 2))
where dmgReduction is a fraction, armorValue is the player's active Armor stat, and stageIndex is the current stage minus 1 (The first stage is index 0).
- Dodge
- Move Speed
- Pickup Radius
- XP Gain
- Luck
- Mining Speed
Weapon Stats (affect all weapons)
- Damage
- Fire Rate
- Reload Speed
- Critical Chance
- Critical Damage
- Status Effect Damage
